#### Lord Krishna's message is timeless, says MLA

**In short:** Mangaluru South MLA D Vedavyas Kamath said Lord Sri Krishna's teachings remain relevant to all sections of society while inaugurating Sri Krishna Janmashtami celebrations at Kudmul Ranga Rao Town Hall organised by the Dakshina Kannada district administration, DK Zilla Panchayat and the Department of Kannada and Culture. MUDA Chairman Santosh Kumar Shetty, Kannada Sahitya Parishat President Srinath, Additional SP Anil Kumar Bhoomireddy and other officials attended the event.

#### Raaga Sudha, an evening dedicated to Hindustani classical music

**In short:** Kala Sadhana, in association with the Ramakrishna Mission, is organising 'Raaga Sudha', an evening of Hindustani classical music at Sri Ramakrishna Mutt near Sri Mangaladevi Temple in Mangaluru on September 6 at 5 pm. Hindustani vocalist Aditya Modak, harmonium artiste Satish Bhat Heggar and tabla artiste Akshay Joshi will perform, with Ramakrishna Mutt president Swami Jitakamananda inaugurating the event.

#### Youngest record holder

**In short:** Joash, aged 7 years and 2 months and the grandson of retired principal Antony A J and Dr Beena of Mangaluru, entered the India Book of Records by identifying 185 breeds of dogs on a laptop screen in 6 minutes and 26 seconds. His parents are Sheetal and Dr Ajoy.

#### Worm allegedly found inside egg: Blinkit facility sealed

**In short:** Food safety officials inspected a Blinkit online retail facility in Mangaluru after a customer alleged that a worm was found inside an egg purchased through the quick-commerce platform, with the complaint reportedly submitted to Health Minister U T Khader. During inspection of the facility at Falnir Square in Falnir, officials found packets containing broken eggs and directed staff to remove and dispose of affected stock; the storage room was temporarily closed and sealed for cleaning and sanitisation, and the Food Safety Department said it would issue a notice seeking an explanation.

Food safety officials inspected a Blinkit online retail facility in Mangaluru after a customer alleged that a worm was found inside an egg purchased through the quick-commerce platform, with the complaint reportedly submitted to Health Minister U T Khader. During inspection of the facility at Falnir Square in Falnir, officials found packets containing broken eggs and directed staff to remove and dispose of affected stock; the storage room was temporarily closed and sealed for cleaning and sanitisation, and the Food Safety Department said it would issue a notice seeking an explanation. In a separate action in Bengaluru, the Department of Food Safety and Drug Administration closed and seized a kitchen at Chairman's Club in Sahakarnagar's Dollars Colony after finding around 40 kg of expired chilli and alleged violations including improper labelling, unhygienic conditions and storage of vegetarian and non-vegetarian food together.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Worm allegedly found inside egg: Blinkit facility sealed
- **Told:** Food safety officials inspected a Blinkit online retail facility in Mangaluru after a customer alleged that a worm was found inside an egg purchased through the quick-commerce platform, with the complaint reportedly submitted to Health Minister U T Khader.
- **Purpose:** In a separate action in Bengaluru, the Department of Food Safety and Drug Administration closed and seized a kitchen at Chairman's Club in Sahakarnagar's Dollars Colony after finding around 40 kg of expired chilli and alleged violations including improper labelling, unhygienic conditions and storage of vegetarian and non-vegetarian food together.

**Important points**

#### 16-coach Vande Bharat to undergo Yeshwanthpur–Mangaluru test run

**In short:** A 16-coach Vande Bharat Express will undertake a trial run between Yeshwanthpur and Mangaluru Central on Saturday, September 5, as directed by the Railway Board. The train will leave Yeshwanthpur at 6.05am and arrive at Mangaluru Central at 2.25pm, halting at Hassan, Sakleshpur, Subrahmanya Road and Padil; it returns from Mangaluru Central at 3pm and reaches Yeshwanthpur at 11pm.

A 16-coach Vande Bharat Express will undertake a trial run between Yeshwanthpur and Mangaluru Central on Saturday, September 5, as directed by the Railway Board. The train will leave Yeshwanthpur at 6.05am and arrive at Mangaluru Central at 2.25pm, halting at Hassan, Sakleshpur, Subrahmanya Road and Padil; it returns from Mangaluru Central at 3pm and reaches Yeshwanthpur at 11pm. The trial assumes importance as the route includes the challenging ghat section, where officials will assess operational performance under varying gradients and explore reducing the proposed journey time of more than eight hours without compromising safety.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** 16-coach Vande Bharat to undergo Yeshwanthpur–Mangaluru test run

#### 3 booked for 'abusing, threatening' students

**In short:** Three persons have been booked for allegedly abusing and threatening four private college students at a cafe near Mangaluru on September 3, police said on Friday. The accused — Rithil Poojary, Prem Bhandari and Sanjay Hegde — allegedly entered the cafe near the Moodbidri bus stand without permission and questioned the students; two of the accused are alleged to be Sangh Parivar activists.

Three persons have been booked for allegedly abusing and threatening four private college students at a cafe near Mangaluru on September 3, police said on Friday. The accused — Rithil Poojary, Prem Bhandari and Sanjay Hegde — allegedly entered the cafe near the Moodbidri bus stand without permission and questioned the students; two of the accused are alleged to be Sangh Parivar activists. Police Sub-Inspector Pratibha filed a complaint and registered a case under Sections 126(2), 352, 351(2) and 3(5) of the Bharatiya Nyaya Sanhita; Bhandari and Hegde had previously figured in four cases at Moodbidri police station and a rowdy sheet has reportedly been opened against them.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** 3 booked for 'abusing, threatening' students

#### FIR against NITK professors over alleged NCC TA/DA fraud

**In short:** Surathkal Police have registered an FIR against two NITK Surathkal professors — Associate NCC Officers Prof P Johnson and Prof H Shivananda — over alleged fraud in NCC TA/DA claim bills based on false documents, according to Deccan Herald. Police have detained the duo and an internal investigation is underway at NITK, where the "2 Kar Engr Coy NCC (Army Wing)" unit has functioned since 1963 with around 160 enrolled students; NITK Director B Ravi said the unit should follow honesty and integrity, and Dr Mohan Lal Meena has been appointed NCC caretaker officer in place of the ANOs.

#### Traffic curbs planned in Moodbidri to ease congestion

**In short:** Authorities have decided to introduce measures to ease traffic congestion in Moodbidri town, including restricting heavy vehicles between 7am and 9pm and issuing MDB identification marks and numbers for autorickshaws operating within the city. Lorries and other heavy vehicles will be diverted through the ring road and outer roads; signboards indicating one-way traffic and no-entry zones will be installed, private buses directed to enter the designated bus stand and adhere to permitted timings, and measures taken to curb overspeeding and inspect horns fitted on vehicles.

Authorities have decided to introduce measures to ease traffic congestion in Moodbidri town, including restricting heavy vehicles between 7am and 9pm and issuing MDB identification marks and numbers for autorickshaws operating within the city. Lorries and other heavy vehicles will be diverted through the ring road and outer roads; signboards indicating one-way traffic and no-entry zones will be installed, private buses directed to enter the designated bus stand and adhere to permitted timings, and measures taken to curb overspeeding and inspect horns fitted on vehicles. The decision followed a meeting attended by ACP Srikanth, municipal chief officer Indu M., inspector Rajendra, transport association representatives and others.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Traffic curbs planned in Moodbidri to ease congestion
- **Told:** Authorities have decided to introduce measures to ease traffic congestion in Moodbidri town, including restricting heavy vehicles between 7am and 9pm and issuing MDB identification marks and numbers for autorickshaws operating within the city.

#### Two NIT-K professors held over fake bills

**In short:** Police arrested two NIT-K Surathkal associate NCC officers — P. Sam Johnson of the Department of Mathematical and Computational Sciences and H.

Police arrested two NIT-K Surathkal associate NCC officers — P. Sam Johnson of the Department of Mathematical and Computational Sciences and H. Shivananda Nayaka of the Department of Mechanical Engineering — for claiming ₹13.51 lakh in travel, accommodation and daily allowance using forged NCC camp documents; director B. Ravi's complaint alleged Johnson swindled ₹12.32 lakh and Nayaka ₹1.18 lakh for duties claimed between May 5, 2023 and May 16, 2026, including ₹1.18 lakh each for a Pre-Thal Sainik Camp-II in Bengaluru from June 28 to July 7, 2025 using the same fake detailment order.

#### Traffic advisory issued ahead of Mosaru Kudike celebrations

**In short:** The Mangaluru City Police on Friday issued a traffic advisory for Mosaru Kudike programmes across the city from Saturday evening on Sri Krishna Janmashtami, urging motorists to avoid congested routes. #### NIT-K introduces revised 'bold and balanced' engineering curriculum

**In short:** The National Institute of Technology-Karnataka (NIT-K), Surathkal, said on Friday that it has introduced a revised, "bold and balanced" engineering curriculum for the BTech batch of 2026, reducing total credits while allowing Honours or Minors programmes. Credits from theory classes will now nearly equal those from practical activities outside the classroom, meaning students will effectively spend two-thirds of their time outside classrooms on laboratory work, life-skills activities, industry internship, R&D project and business startup planning.

The National Institute of Technology-Karnataka (NIT-K), Surathkal, said on Friday that it has introduced a revised, "bold and balanced" engineering curriculum for the BTech batch of 2026, reducing total credits while allowing Honours or Minors programmes. Credits from theory classes will now nearly equal those from practical activities outside the classroom, meaning students will effectively spend two-thirds of their time outside classrooms on laboratory work, life-skills activities, industry internship, R&D project and business startup planning. Director B. Ravi said the institute aims to prepare students for the AI era and produce well-rounded individuals contributing to Viksit Bharat by 2047.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** NIT-K introduces revised 'bold and balanced' engineering curriculum
- **Told:** The National Institute of Technology-Karnataka (NIT-K), Surathkal, said on Friday that it has introduced a revised, "bold and balanced" engineering curriculum for the BTech batch of 2026, reducing total credits while allowing Honours or Minors programmes.
- **Purpose:** Ravi said the institute aims to prepare students for the AI era and produce well-rounded individuals contributing to Viksit Bharat by 2047.

**Important points**

#### Temple town immerses in devotional fervour during Janmashtami celebrations

**In short:** Thousands of devotees gathered at Sri Krishna Mutt in Udupi as the sacred Arghya Pradana ritual marking Lord Krishna's birth was performed at 12:12 AM on September 5 at Seetha Mantap, led by Shiroor Mutt seer Sri Vedavardhana Theertha Swami. Car Street was decorated with 13 triangular wooden Gurjis and two large pavilions, with 47 earthen pots suspended for traditional Mosaru Kudike and Vittla Pindi celebrations scheduled for Saturday.

#### Two chosen for best teacher award

**In short:** Two teachers from Dakshina Kannada and Udupi districts have been selected for the State-level Best Teacher Award for 2026-27. Ashoka, a teacher at Government Higher Primary School, Kundapur taluk, Udupi district, is among 20 primary school teachers selected for the State award, while Roshan Alexander Pinto, Assistant Teacher, Grade II, Carmel High School, Bantwal taluk, Dakshina Kannada district, is among 11 high school teachers chosen.

Two teachers from Dakshina Kannada and Udupi districts have been selected for the State-level Best Teacher Award for 2026-27. Ashoka, a teacher at Government Higher Primary School, Kundapur taluk, Udupi district, is among 20 primary school teachers selected for the State award, while Roshan Alexander Pinto, Assistant Teacher, Grade II, Carmel High School, Bantwal taluk, Dakshina Kannada district, is among 11 high school teachers chosen. Seven high school teachers from Dakshina Kannada have been chosen for the district-level award — Vijayalakshmi, Ravindra K., Praveen Cutinho, Kishore, John Ronald D'Souza, M. I. Thomas and Kishore Kumar — and seven lower primary and seven higher primary teachers from Dakshina Kannada have also been selected for district-level awards.

#### SIO organises inter-college history fair in Bhatkal

**In short:** The Students Islamic Organisation of India (SIO), Bhatkal unit, organised an inter-college history fair titled "Imprints of the Past" at Shams School in Bhatkal, with 12 teams from pre-university and degree colleges presenting historical models. The fair was inaugurated by Maulana Syed Zubair SM, Ameer of Jamaat-e-Islami Hind, Bhatkal; chief guest Nihal Kidiyoor, former secretary of SIO of India, urged students to develop a deeper understanding of history.

#### 'Allow outstation voters' kin to respond to SIR notices'

**In short:** Majlis-e-Islah-wa-Tanzeem, a century-old social organisation in Bhatkal, has urged Karnataka authorities to allow adult family members to respond to Special Intensive Revision (SIR) notices and submit required documents on behalf of voters residing abroad or outside the state. General Secretary Azizur Rahman Ruknuddin Nadvi met Uttara Kannada Deputy Commissioner K.

Majlis-e-Islah-wa-Tanzeem, a century-old social organisation in Bhatkal, has urged Karnataka authorities to allow adult family members to respond to Special Intensive Revision (SIR) notices and submit required documents on behalf of voters residing abroad or outside the state. General Secretary Azizur Rahman Ruknuddin Nadvi met Uttara Kannada Deputy Commissioner K. Lakshmi Priya in Karwar and submitted a memorandum, citing a large number of Bhatkal voters living abroad or outside Karnataka and a Telangana Chief Electoral Officer directive of August 27, 2026 permitting adult family members aged above 18 to appear before EROs or AEROs on behalf of senior citizens, persons with disabilities, those who are ill and overseas voters.

#### No end to cyber fraud: ₹26 crore lost in two and a half years

**In short:** Despite sustained awareness campaigns, cyber fraud continues to pose a serious threat in Uttara Kannada, with fraudsters allegedly siphoning off nearly ₹26 crore from residents over the past two and a half years. According to the state Home Department, 237 cyber fraud cases were registered at the Uttara Kannada cyber police station during the period, 14 accused were arrested, and only around ₹1.85 crore could be recovered; fraudsters have used fake online job offers, stock market investment scams, impersonation of bank or government officials, KYC update requests and social media targeting.

Despite sustained awareness campaigns, cyber fraud continues to pose a serious threat in Uttara Kannada, with fraudsters allegedly siphoning off nearly ₹26 crore from residents over the past two and a half years. According to the state Home Department, 237 cyber fraud cases were registered at the Uttara Kannada cyber police station during the period, 14 accused were arrested, and only around ₹1.85 crore could be recovered; fraudsters have used fake online job offers, stock market investment scams, impersonation of bank or government officials, KYC update requests and social media targeting. The Uttara Kannada police have announced a video competition on cyber safety, with submissions due to the district police social media cell by September 20.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** No end to cyber fraud: ₹26 crore lost in two and a half years
- **Told:** Despite sustained awareness campaigns, cyber fraud continues to pose a serious threat in Uttara Kannada, with fraudsters allegedly siphoning off nearly ₹26 crore from residents over the past two and a half years.
- **Purpose:** The Uttara Kannada police have announced a video competition on cyber safety, with submissions due to the district police social media cell by September 20.

**Important points**
- The Uttara Kannada police have announced a video competition on cyber safety, with submissions due to the district police social media cell by September 20.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.3_

### Karnataka

#### Tunnel may damage Lalbagh rock, warns GSI

**In short:** A Geological Survey of India expert committee has flagged serious geological, structural and groundwater risks to Lalbagh and its three-billion-year-old Lalbagh rock in a 27-page report submitted to the government in April. The committee warned that tunneling and excavation could damage the ancient rock mass, widen existing cracks and threaten the Kempegowda tower, and flagged proposed Shaft 4 adjacent to the National Geological Monument as a critical concern.

A Geological Survey of India expert committee has flagged serious geological, structural and groundwater risks to Lalbagh and its three-billion-year-old Lalbagh rock in a 27-page report submitted to the government in April. The committee warned that tunneling and excavation could damage the ancient rock mass, widen existing cracks and threaten the Kempegowda tower, and flagged proposed Shaft 4 adjacent to the National Geological Monument as a critical concern. The findings are significant as the state government was on the verge of awarding the Rs 22,267-crore, 16.75-km Hebbal–Central Silk Board tunnel road project to Adani Enterprises, the lowest bidder.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Tunnel may damage Lalbagh rock, warns GSI
- **Told:** A Geological Survey of India expert committee has flagged serious geological, structural and groundwater risks to Lalbagh and its three-billion-year-old Lalbagh rock in a 27-page report submitted to the government in April.
- **Purpose:** The findings are significant as the state government was on the verge of awarding the Rs 22,267-crore, 16.75-km Hebbal–Central Silk Board tunnel road project to Adani Enterprises, the lowest bidder.

**Important points**
- The findings are significant as the state government was on the verge of awarding the Rs 22,267-crore, 16.75-km Hebbal–Central Silk Board tunnel road project to Adani Enterprises, the lowest bidder.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.1; Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.4_

#### Govt vehicles violate traffic rules, pile up over 82k cases in 3 years

**In short:** Karnataka government vehicles, including police and non-police department fleets, accumulated over 82,000 traffic violation cases between 2023 and July 2026 with Rs 19.56 crore in unpaid penalties, data presented in the Legislative Council showed. Authorities recovered only Rs 47.72 lakh, leaving over 97% unpaid, with Mysuru city recording 28,708 cases against non-police government vehicles in 2024; Home Minister Priyank Kharge said individual drivers, not the government exchequer, must pay the fines.

#### Stop tunnel projects in Maha, Delhi, DKS dares BJP

**In short:** Chief Minister D K Shivakumar on Friday defended the Bengaluru tunnel road project and challenged the BJP to stop similar tunnel road projects in Maharashtra and Delhi before criticising Karnataka's plans. Reacting to the withdrawal of the Karnataka Government Parks (Preservation) (Amendment) Bill, he denied running a U-turn government and said he is open to alternatives, noting the withdrawn amendment would have allowed alienation of up to 5% of park or garden land for infrastructure.

#### Yellappa Reddy offers to work with govt over alternatives for tunnel road

**In short:** Environmentalist A N Yellappa Reddy, who led the public campaign against the Parks Act amendment, has written to Chief Minister D K Shivakumar offering to suggest alternatives to the proposed tunnel road project in Bengaluru. Following the cabinet's decision on Thursday to withdraw the Karnataka Government Parks (Preservation) (Amendment) Act, 2026, Reddy said the government must find environmentally responsible solutions to Bengaluru's mobility challenges.

#### After Parks Bill, BJP plans to oppose tanks and BMLTA amendments

**In short:** Following the withdrawal of the Parks Bill, the opposition BJP announced plans to oppose amendments to the Karnataka Tank Conservation and Development Authority Act and the Bengaluru Metropolitan Land Transport Authority Act, accusing the government of tabling three bills to facilitate two tunnel road projects. Union Minister Shobha Karandlaje and Bangalore South MP Tejasvi Surya said the Tank Conservation amendment seeks to replace the 30-metre buffer zone around water bodies with varying zones to acquire 3.5 acres of Hebbal Lake land for the Rs 40,000-crore project.

#### Hanur shootout: Suspect releases one more video

**In short:** Mahima Das, a suspect who escaped a shootout with the Forest Department at Cauvery Wildlife Sanctuary on August 15 in which three people were killed, released a video clip claiming forest officials fired first while he and three others were resting in Karikallu Gudda forest. Das said he was 500 feet away when officials opened fire, escaped into the woods after being shot at and later admitted himself to a hospital, and claimed the group entered the forest for fishing, not poaching.

#### K'taka SIR: Over 43.81L notices generated, 7.31L delivered

**In short:** Over 43.81 lakh notices have been generated in Karnataka's ongoing Special Intensive Revision (SIR) of electoral rolls, of which 7.31 lakh have been delivered, the chief electoral officer's office said on Friday. The state has 4,46,38,124 electors as of September 4 — 2.21 crore male, 2.24 crore female and 2,871 transgender — with notices issued to electors in "no mapping" and "anomalies" categories; 36,49,371 notices remain pending delivery and 206 hearings have been completed since hearings began on September 3 and run until October 12.

Over 43.81 lakh notices have been generated in Karnataka's ongoing Special Intensive Revision (SIR) of electoral rolls, of which 7.31 lakh have been delivered, the chief electoral officer's office said on Friday. The state has 4,46,38,124 electors as of September 4 — 2.21 crore male, 2.24 crore female and 2,871 transgender — with notices issued to electors in "no mapping" and "anomalies" categories; 36,49,371 notices remain pending delivery and 206 hearings have been completed since hearings began on September 3 and run until October 12. A second special registration campaign will be held on September 19–20, and electors unable to find their names after polling-station rationalisation are asked to search by EPIC number on voters.eci.gov.in or the ECINET app.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** K'taka SIR: Over 43.81L notices generated, 7.31L delivered
- **Told:** Over 43.81 lakh notices have been generated in Karnataka's ongoing Special Intensive Revision (SIR) of electoral rolls, of which 7.31 lakh have been delivered, the chief electoral officer's office said on Friday.

#### State turns to cloud seeding amid worsening drought

**In short:** Amid drought conditions, the Karnataka government on Friday launched a cloud-seeding programme at HAL airport, using a specially equipped light aircraft at an estimated cost of ₹28.52 crore. Water Resources Minister N.

Amid drought conditions, the Karnataka government on Friday launched a cloud-seeding programme at HAL airport, using a specially equipped light aircraft at an estimated cost of ₹28.52 crore. Water Resources Minister N. Cheluvarayaswamy said an earlier operation had a success rate of only 35%, but every effort to augment water availability mattered as the State had received little rainfall and the southwest monsoon would conclude by the end of this month, warning that without adequate drinking water stored by then scarcity could persist until next June. A technical advisory committee will update information every two hours and aircraft will be deployed to rainfall-deficit areas, reservoir catchments and distressed districts when suitable clouds are detected using Doppler radar.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** State turns to cloud seeding amid worsening drought
- **Told:** Amid drought conditions, the Karnataka government on Friday launched a cloud-seeding programme at HAL airport, using a specially equipped light aircraft at an estimated cost of ₹28.52 crore.
- **Purpose:** A technical advisory committee will update information every two hours and aircraft will be deployed to rainfall-deficit areas, reservoir catchments and distressed districts when suitable clouds are detected using Doppler radar.

**Important points**
- Cheluvarayaswamy said an earlier operation had a success rate of only 35%, but every effort to augment water availability mattered as the State had received little rainfall and the southwest monsoon would conclude by the end of this month, warning that without adequate drinking water stored by then scarcity could persist until next June.
- A technical advisory committee will update information every two hours and aircraft will be deployed to rainfall-deficit areas, reservoir catchments and distressed districts when suitable clouds are detected using Doppler radar.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.4; The Hindu Mangaluru p.3; The Hindu Mangaluru p.6_

#### Not the right time: IMD scientists

**In short:** India Meteorological Department scientists expressed scepticism about the timing of Karnataka's cloud seeding operation, saying there is currently not enough condensed moisture in the clouds for the process to be effective. They suggested seeding would be more successful after October during the North East monsoon.

India Meteorological Department scientists expressed scepticism about the timing of Karnataka's cloud seeding operation, saying there is currently not enough condensed moisture in the clouds for the process to be effective. #### Crop loss study in 24 more taluks of 11 districts

**In short:** The state government ordered ground-truthing in 24 additional taluks across 11 districts including Dharwad, Vijayapura, Raichur, Kodagu, Chikkamagaluru, Shivamogga, Haveri, Bidar, Koppal and Tumakuru to assess drought severity following farmer protests, with a final report expected by September 11. Taluks may be declared moderately drought-affected if crop loss exceeds 33% or severely affected if loss exceeds 50%, after 101 taluks were already identified as drought-hit.

#### 'Prosecution sanction files are with me'

**In short:** Chief Minister D K Shivakumar said he has received files from Governor Thaawarchand Gehlot's office regarding prosecution sanctions against accused B Nagendra and at least two BJP leaders including Janardhana Reddy and M P Renukacharya, but has not yet opened or examined them legally. Lok Bhavan has asked the government to recommend either sanctioning or rejecting permission to prosecute.

#### Criminal cases will be filed against lake encroachers: Khandre

**In short:** Rural Development and Panchayat Raj Minister Eshwar Khandre has warned that any encroachment of lakes belonging to and maintained by gram panchayats will be cleared and criminal cases filed against encroachers, saying Karnataka has 32,504 such lakes, of which 11,498 have been encroached upon, with encroachments cleared from 6,670 lakes but persisting in 4,828 lakes covering 11,671 acres. He said Karnataka is the second most drought-prone State with declining groundwater, that surface water sources must be protected, and that Karnataka is the first State to undertake water budgeting and has implemented continuous drinking water supply in 268 villages yielding a 7% saving.

He said Karnataka is the second most drought-prone State with declining groundwater, that surface water sources must be protected, and that Karnataka is the first State to undertake water budgeting and has implemented continuous drinking water supply in 268 villages yielding a 7% saving.

He said Karnataka is the second most drought-prone State with declining groundwater, that surface water sources must be protected, and that Karnataka is the first State to undertake water budgeting and has implemented continuous drinking water supply in 268 villages yielding a 7% saving.

#### BJP circular allows 7% cut in KRIDL: Khandre

**In short:** Forest Minister Eshwar Khandre alleged that a circular issued during the previous BJP government allowed a 7% cut in Karnataka Rural Infrastructure Development Limited funds and formed a committee headed by a superintending engineer to probe corruption allegations, expected to submit a report within seven days. #### K'taka to collect DNA samples of kin of missing Nepal tourists

**In short:** The Karnataka government is collecting DNA samples from first-degree relatives of 26 people from the state, including 17 Bengalureans, who remain untraceable after the August 26 floods in Nepal, coordinating with the Ministry of External Affairs to match samples with mortal remains found there. Missing persons named include Hrishikesh HS, Reena Kathandaraman and Nallaipalli Ramakrishnan from Whitefield and 44-year-old S Kuzhanthaivelan.

#### School ceiling collapse: Lokayukta cops register suo motu case

**In short:** Lokayukta police in Bagalkot registered a suo motu case after a student was injured when a concrete ceiling collapsed at a government higher primary school in Mugalolli Tanda, issuing notices to high-ranking officials in the Education and PWD departments. The Lokayukta directed inspection of other government school buildings and ordered a safety audit of all school buildings in the state.

#### Bowring Hospital to verify DNA samples

**In short:** Bowring and Lady Curzon Hospital in Bengaluru has been designated the nodal centre for collecting and verifying DNA samples from relatives of Karnataka victims missing in Nepal, with Dr Nagesh Kuppast (9742707500) as nodal officer. Relatives are advised to bring government-issued ID such as Aadhaar and recent photographs of the victims.

#### RSS march: KSAT stays suspension of two Yadgir teachers

**In short:** The Kalaburagi bench of the Karnataka State Administrative Tribunal stayed the suspension of headmaster Anna Saheb and assistant teacher Gururaj Joshi from Yadgir, suspended by the DDPI for participating in an RSS Patha Sanchalana march in October last year. The tribunal held that participation in a programme by a non-political entity does not violate Karnataka Civil Services (Conduct) Rules, 2021, and noted no official order prohibits officials from RSS events, staying the order until October 9.

#### Industry participation crucial for PM-SETU scheme: Minister

**In short:** Karnataka Industries Minister MB Patil on Friday urged public and private sector industries to participate in the PM-SETU scheme upgrading 48 Industrial Training Institutes under a hub-and-spoke model covering 12 hubs with four ITIs each. In Bengaluru Urban, Peenya ITI will serve as the hub with Tumakuru Road, Domlur, Hosur Road and Devanahalli as spokes; in Kalaburagi, the Government ITI for Boys will be the hub with Sedam, Afzalpur, Jewargi and the Government ITI for Women as spokes, with a Special Purpose Vehicle including six industry representatives and eligibility for companies with at least Rs 500 crore turnover and 1,000 employees.

#### Nostalgia to return to screens

**In short:** Nagathihalli Chandrashekhar's 1997 Kannada film 'America America', the first Kannada film screened widely outside India and a National Award winner that ran over a year at Bengaluru's Abhinaya theatre, gets a sequel 'America America 2' releasing September 11. Adapted from Sahana Vijay Kumar's novel 'Kshame' rather than continuing the original love story, the sequel juxtaposes serene Mysuru against turbulent Seattle to examine whether women's economic independence over three decades has translated into happiness and how interpretations of love and relationships have changed.

#### Shreeniti in line for double

**In short:** Fourth seed Shreeniti Chowdhury took out Vasundra Balajee 3-6, 6-1, 6-1 to reach the girls' singles final of the KSLTA World Tennis Tour Junior J30 Bengaluru at the S.M. Krishna Tennis Stadium here on Friday.

Fourth seed Shreeniti Chowdhury took out Vasundra Balajee 3-6, 6-1, 6-1 to reach the girls' singles final of the KSLTA World Tennis Tour Junior J30 Bengaluru at the S.M. Krishna Tennis Stadium here on Friday. Shreeniti also reached the doubles final alongside Riddhi Shinde. In the boys' semifinal, Fazal Ali Meer edged out Vivaan Mirdha 6-4, 1-6, 7-5; Niyanth Badrinarayanan (USA) beat Tejas Ravi 7-6(6), 6-4, and Aahida Singh beat Zoha Qureshi 6-2, 6-0 in the other girls' semifinal.

#### Asiad-bound athletes get a taste of Japan in Bengaluru

**In short:** SAI Bengaluru is acclimatising Asian Games-bound athletes including Karnataka pole vaulter G Sindhushree and Supriya B with Japanese cuisine such as sushi, unagi and uramaki, a simulator container room replicating compact Japan accommodation, and a pamphlet on greetings, transport, currency and etiquette. Chef V Jeyaraj's kitchen feeds around 350 athletes daily; regional director Vishnu Sudhakaran said the food programme began in January and expanded once the final squad was known in July ahead of Aichi-Nagoya from September 19 to October 4.

#### KSCA President's XI scores thrilling win

**In short:** KSCA President's XI recorded an exciting one-wicket win over Goa CA in their Dr. (Capt.) K.

KSCA President's XI recorded an exciting one-wicket win over Goa CA in their Dr. (Capt.) K. Thimappiah Memorial tournament match at the M. Chinnaswamy Stadium here on Friday. Set a target of 150, KSCA President's XI fell to 100 for eight when Anvay Dravid (30) was dismissed, but tailenders Manvanth Kumar (18), Shikhar Shetty (28 n.o.) and Mohsin Khan (six n.o.) held firm to guide the side to a win; Goa CA made 338 and 207 while KSCA President's XI replied with 396 and 151/9 in 42.1 overs (Ruthvik Nai 5/32). At Alur, spinners Paras Gurbax Arya (four for 40) and Dheeraj Gowda (five for 19) powered KSCA Secretary's XI past Baroda CA, and at Mysuru left-arm spinner Saurabh Kumar (five for 80), supported by medium-pacer Madhav Rayudu (three for 43), enabled Andhra CA to register a 10-run win over Himachal Pradesh CA.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** KSCA President's XI scores thrilling win
- **Told:** KSCA President's XI recorded an exciting one-wicket win over Goa CA in their Dr.
- **Purpose:** At Alur, spinners Paras Gurbax Arya (four for 40) and Dheeraj Gowda (five for 19) powered KSCA Secretary's XI past Baroda CA, and at Mysuru left-arm spinner Saurabh Kumar (five for 80), supported by medium-pacer Madhav Rayudu (three for 43), enabled Andhra CA to register a 10-run win over Himachal Pradesh CA.

**Important points**
- Thimappiah Memorial tournament match at the M.

- KSCA President's XI fell to 100 for eight when Anvay Dravid (30) was dismissed, but tailenders Manvanth Kumar (18).

#### SIR in K'taka: 43.81 lakh notice generated

**In short:** Over 43.81 lakh notices have been generated in Karnataka's ongoing SIR of electoral rolls, of which 7.31 lakh have been delivered to electors, the chief electoral officer's office said Friday. The State has 4,46,38,124 electors as of September 4 — 2.21 crore male, 2.24 crore female and 2,871 transgender — with notices issued to those in "no mapping" and "anomalies" categories; 43,81,141 of 43,81,143 planned notices have been generated, 36,49,371 remain pending delivery, and 206 hearings completed.

#### Karnataka likely to revise government bus fares soon, says minister

**In short:** Transport Minister B. Suresh said the Karnataka government is preparing to revise state-run bus fares after refraining from increases despite rising fuel costs over eight months, with the four transport corporations suffering losses running into thousands of crores.

Transport Minister B. Suresh said the Karnataka government is preparing to revise state-run bus fares after refraining from increases despite rising fuel costs over eight months, with the four transport corporations suffering losses running into thousands of crores. A committee headed by retired IAS officer Atul Tiwari is studying the issue and expected to submit its report shortly; the minister said any increase would be moderate given the drought situation. The government also plans free Shakti scheme smart cards for over one crore women at around ₹250 crore, BMTC procurement of 4,500 electric buses under PM E-DRIVE (3,800 twelve-metre and 900 nine-metre, about 400 air-conditioned) at roughly ₹600 crore annually, and a ₹2,000 crore Centre loan for new depots and upgraded bus stands.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Karnataka likely to revise government bus fares soon, says minister
- **Told:** Transport Minister B.
- **Purpose:** The government also plans free Shakti scheme smart cards for over one crore women at around ₹250 crore, BMTC procurement of 4,500 electric buses under PM E-DRIVE (3,800 twelve-metre and 900 nine-metre, about 400 air-conditioned) at roughly ₹600 crore annually, and a ₹2,000 crore Centre loan for new depots and upgraded bus stands.

**Important points**
- A committee headed by retired IAS officer Atul Tiwari is studying the issue and expected to submit its report shortly.
- the minister said any increase would be moderate given the drought situation.
- The government also plans free Shakti scheme smart cards for over one crore women at around ₹250 crore.
- BMTC procurement of 4,500 electric buses under PM E-DRIVE (3,800 twelve-metre and 900 nine-metre, about 400 air-conditioned) at roughly ₹600 crore annually, and a ₹2,000 crore Centre loan for new depots and upgraded bus stands.

#### CoA awards on Sept 8

**In short:** Sir MV School of Architecture will host the Council of Architecture (CoA) National Awards for Excellence in Architectural Thesis for 2026 from September 8 to 10. This year's exhibits showcase projects on the revival of Art and Heritage of India, focusing on the Deccan region, and the event includes a closed jury of selected thesis projects from 100-plus institutions, a public heritage documentation exhibition, thesis presentations, and cultural and valedictory programmes.

Sir MV School of Architecture will host the Council of Architecture (CoA) National Awards for Excellence in Architectural Thesis for 2026 from September 8 to 10. #### Three arrested for hunting two deer using electric wire

**In short:** Forest Department officials have arrested three persons — Banu, Shivu and Mahesh, residents of Nagavalli village and accused Nos. 2, 5 and 6 — for allegedly hunting two deer by electrifying a wire in the Sharavati Wildlife Sanctuary in Sagar taluk of Shivamogga district.

Forest Department officials have arrested three persons — Banu, Shivu and Mahesh, residents of Nagavalli village and accused Nos. 2, 5 and 6 — for allegedly hunting two deer by electrifying a wire in the Sharavati Wildlife Sanctuary in Sagar taluk of Shivamogga district. The accused allegedly laid an electrified wire in farmland belonging to Shaji, electrocuted two male deer, butchered them for meat and buried skins and bones underground; three other accused — Shaji, Rama and Sridhar — are reportedly absconding, and officials are examining the source of electricity and whether similar offences occurred earlier.

#### No time: Carlsen urges chess to look beyond him

**In short:** Five-time world champion Magnus Carlsen, speaking in Bengaluru ahead of the fourth Global Chess League where he will represent Alpine APL Pipers, said organisers should not shape chess's future around his preferences because his time at the top is "limited." Carlsen, who holds the highest rating in chess history at 2882 and leads the FIDE rankings, has opted out of the classical World Championship cycle but remains eager to compete in rapid formats like the GCL. He said new fans and formats over the last five to six years are interesting to be part of, but his motivation fluctuates tournament to tournament and the sport should innovate beyond what motivates him alone.

#### Notification granting externment powers to SPs struck down by HC

**In short:** The High Court of Karnataka has struck down a notification issued on November 18, 2025 empowering Superintendents of Police in districts to pass externment orders under the Karnataka Police Act, 1963, holding that Section 55 empowers only the Police Commissioner in commissionerate cities, the District Magistrate elsewhere, and a specially empowered Sub-Divisional Magistrate. Justice Sachin Shankar Magadum observed the SP does not fall within categories contemplated by Section 55 and set aside an April 2026 externment order passed by the Belagavi SP against Chandrakant Shankar Vaddar, a 54-year-old labourer from Nippani, clarifying executive instructions cannot override express statutory provisions.

#### Prabhu Deva invited to inaugurate Yuva Dasara

**In short:** Film director, actor and choreographer Prabhu Deva has been invited to inaugurate this year's Yuva Dasara in Mysuru. Yuva Dasara, organised as part of the Mysuru Dasara festivities, is one of the most popular events of the celebrations, attracting people of all age groups, particularly the youth, with music and dance performances and concerts by renowned artists on a large vacant plot in recent years to accommodate growing crowds.

#### Praja Seva meetings to be held at taluk, hobli levels on Saturdays

**In short:** The Praja Seva Abhiyan, to be launched across the State on Saturday through the Department of Prajaseva, aims to bring the administration to people's doorsteps every Saturday, with taluk and hobli meetings on the first and third Saturdays under the District In-charge Minister and on the second and fourth under the local MLA. The public will be informed at least 15 days in advance through pamphlets, pre-printed application forms and AI-based outbound calls; GramaOne kiosks will accept applications at venues, with separate queues for new grievances and for UPGRS cases unresolved or inadequately addressed after 21 days, and eligible beneficiaries may receive sanction or redressal letters on the spot.

#### Complaints, applications received at meetings to be reviewed in three stages

**In short:** All applications and complaints received during the weekly Praja Seva Abhiyan will be systematically reviewed on Wednesdays in three stages: taluk-level review from 9 a.m. to 11 a.m.

All applications and complaints received during the weekly Praja Seva Abhiyan will be systematically reviewed on Wednesdays in three stages: taluk-level review from 9 a.m. to 11 a.m. by Tahsildars and Taluk Panchayat Executive Officers, sub-divisional review from 11 a.m. to 1 p.m. by Assistant Commissioners, and district-level review from 3 p.m. to 5 p.m. by Deputy Commissioners, with dissatisfied citizens able to appear in person at each stage. Applications not disposed of within the specified period will be automatically transferred to the head of the concerned department, a District In-charge Secretary will visit each district on the first and third Friday of every month, and progress will be reviewed by the Chief Secretary and Chief Minister.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Complaints, applications received at meetings to be reviewed in three stages
- **Told:** All applications and complaints received during the weekly Praja Seva Abhiyan will be systematically reviewed on Wednesdays in three stages: taluk-level review from 9 a.m.
- **Purpose:** Applications not disposed of within the specified period will be automatically transferred to the head of the concerned department, a District In-charge Secretary will visit each district on the first and third Friday of every month, and progress will be reviewed by the Chief Secretary and Chief Minister.

**Important points**
- by Tahsildars and Taluk Panchayat Executive Officers, sub-divisional review from 11 a.m.
- to 1 p.m.
- by Assistant Commissioners, and district-level review from 3 p.m.
- to 5 p.m.

#### 32, including 7 women, rescued from bonded labour in sugarcane fields

**In short:** Thirty-two persons, including seven women, were rescued from alleged bonded labour at a sugarcane field in Ganganadoddi village in Chamarajanagar district on Tuesday following a tip-off from the Village Administrative Officer of Suleripalya Circle in Hanur taluk. Shanmugam has been identified as the main accused in an FIR registered at Ramapura police station; he allegedly brought 30 migrant labourers from Tamil Nadu and one each from Andhra Pradesh and Bihar, with associates Ruby, Ranjitha, Sarvanam and Karthik supervising and assaulting them.

Thirty-two persons, including seven women, were rescued from alleged bonded labour at a sugarcane field in Ganganadoddi village in Chamarajanagar district on Tuesday following a tip-off from the Village Administrative Officer of Suleripalya Circle in Hanur taluk. Shanmugam has been identified as the main accused in an FIR registered at Ramapura police station; he allegedly brought 30 migrant labourers from Tamil Nadu and one each from Andhra Pradesh and Bihar, with associates Ruby, Ranjitha, Sarvanam and Karthik supervising and assaulting them. Twelve rescued labourers received pain-relieving injections, three were treated for injuries, and one with a dislocated left elbow was referred to Kollegal hospital; the rescued are housed at the Government Students' Hostel in Hanur, three accused are in custody, and Assistant Commissioner Dinesh Kumar Meena issued Release Certificates on Friday.

**What this is**

**In short:** Three students of the government lower primary school at Kiruvase near Karur in Sagara taluk, Shivamogga district, suffered bruises allegedly after being assaulted by guest teacher Rakshitha on Thursday. #### CM invites BJP leaders to join him in seeking farm loan waiver from Centre

**In short:** Chief Minister D.K. Shivakumar on Friday urged the Centre to waive farm loans in Karnataka in view of the drought and called upon BJP leaders to join him in pressing the demand in New Delhi, saying the State has declared 101 taluks as drought-affected for the kharif season following a deficit monsoon.

Chief Minister D.K. Shivakumar on Friday urged the Centre to waive farm loans in Karnataka in view of the drought and called upon BJP leaders to join him in pressing the demand in New Delhi, saying the State has declared 101 taluks as drought-affected for the kharif season following a deficit monsoon. Speaking at Kudal Sangam in Bagalkot after a drought review, he reiterated Karnataka is facing one of the worst droughts in decades, pointed out BJP State president B.Y. Vijayendra had demanded a farm loan waiver, and urged Mr. Vijayendra and Leader of Opposition R. Ashok to accompany him to Delhi; drought-hit taluks were listed as per Central calamity relief guidelines and the legislature will hold a special session from September 21 to 23 on the drought, agrarian crisis and the Kasturirangan report.

#### Battle of the brains in Garden City

**In short:** The fourth edition of the Global Chess League — a joint venture of Tech Mahindra and FIDE — opens at the Lalit Ashok in Bengaluru on Saturday, bringing the world's leading chess players to the Garden City for the second time the league has been held in India after Season-3 in Mumbai; innovations such as no increment for the first 40 moves, four points for a win with black, mixed men's and women's teams, and a pre-match toss have drawn top players including Magnus Carlsen, whose Alpine Pipers are defending champions, and Viswanathan Anand, Alireza Firouzja, Javokhir Sindarov, Maxime Vachier-Lagrave and Ian Nepomniachtchi.

#### Dasara CM Cup from October 11 to 14

**In short:** The State-level Dasara CM Cup 2026 sports meet will be held at the Chamundivihar Stadium Sports Complex and other venues in Mysuru from October 11 to 14. The taluk-level meet will be held from Saturday, the district-level event will commence on September 18 and the divisional-level meet will be conducted on September 30, with winning athletes at the divisional level qualifying for the State-level meet.

The State-level Dasara CM Cup 2026 sports meet will be held at the Chamundivihar Stadium Sports Complex and other venues in Mysuru from October 11 to 14. The taluk-level meet will be held from Saturday, the district-level event will commence on September 18 and the divisional-level meet will be conducted on September 30, with winning athletes at the divisional level qualifying for the State-level meet. At the State-level championship, athletes from Bengaluru Urban, Bengaluru Rural, Kalburgi, Mysuru and Belagavi will compete in 28 different disciplines.

#### Assault on NEET protester's dad: Hindutva activist held

**In short:** Hindutva activist Swatantra Bhardwaj, who claimed he cracked the skull of protester Nishu Azad's father Sanjay Kumar during the students' agitation at Jantar Mantar on June 23 and later recanted fearing legal trouble, was detained in Uttar Pradesh's Bulandshahr on Friday. The detention came hours after Delhi Police promised activists of the Cockroach Janta Party, Youth Congress and Lok Sabha MP Chandrasekhar that he would be put behind bars within 72 hours; Bhardwaj, 24, initially evaded police teams before being nabbed.

#### Air pollution, biofilms and the lung

**In short:** Bengaluru's worsening air quality in late 2025, with AQI levels around 150–180, joins Delhi research led by JNU's Professor Kasturi Mukhopadhyay finding antibiotic-resistant bacteria in polluted air, raising concern that PM2.5 may carry resistant microbes into lungs and worsen COPD affecting over 55 million Indians, many non-smokers. Scientists including Dr Ajay Kalanjana Monnappa of Bangalore Bioinnovation Centre and Prof.

Bengaluru's worsening air quality in late 2025, with AQI levels around 150–180, joins Delhi research led by JNU's Professor Kasturi Mukhopadhyay finding antibiotic-resistant bacteria in polluted air, raising concern that PM2.5 may carry resistant microbes into lungs and worsen COPD affecting over 55 million Indians, many non-smokers. Scientists including Dr Ajay Kalanjana Monnappa of Bangalore Bioinnovation Centre and Prof. Mamatha Ballal of KMC Manipal explain how biofilms—protective bacterial communities—may persist in damaged airways, resist antibiotics, and link to lung cancer risk, while a 2015 Bangalore hospital study found 49.45% of Staphylococci isolates could form biofilms with strong ties to multidrug resistance.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Air pollution, biofilms and the lung
- **Told:** Bengaluru's worsening air quality in late 2025, with AQI levels around 150–180, joins Delhi research led by JNU's Professor Kasturi Mukhopadhyay finding antibiotic-resistant bacteria in polluted air, raising concern that PM2.5 may carry resistant microbes into lungs and worsen COPD affecting over 55 million Indians, many non-smokers.
- **Purpose:** Mamatha Ballal of KMC Manipal explain how biofilms—protective bacterial communities—may persist in damaged airways, resist antibiotics, and link to lung cancer risk, while a 2015 Bangalore hospital study found 49.45% of Staphylococci isolates could form biofilms with strong ties to multidrug resistance.

**Important points**
- Mamatha Ballal of KMC Manipal explain how biofilms—protective bacterial communities—may persist in damaged airways, resist antibiotics, and link to lung cancer risk, while a 2015 Bangalore hospital study found 49.45% of Staphylococci isolates could form biofilms with strong ties to multidrug resistance.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.7_

#### Centre to hold fresh Ladakh talks on September 9

**In short:** The Central government will hold a fresh round of talks with Ladakh representatives in New Delhi on September 9, three weeks after the August 19 meeting in Leh ended without a breakthrough on the political framework sought by the Leh Apex Body and Kargil Democratic Alliance. Ladakh Chief Secretary Ashish Kundra confirmed the MHA subcommittee meeting; the groups' "non-negotiable" demands include a directly elected Legislative Assembly headed by a Chief Minister and Council of Ministers, substantial control over finances and law and order, a separate Public Service Commission, and constitutional safeguards drawing on provisions available to states such as Nagaland, Sikkim and Mizoram.

#### Raise judicial officers' retiring age, 7 states told

**In short:** The Supreme Court directed seven consenting states—Chhattisgarh, Karnataka, Madhya Pradesh, Maharashtra, Sikkim, Tamil Nadu and West Bengal—to amend service rules and raise judicial officers' retirement age from 60 to 62 years, preferably within two months, subject to high court suitability assessment at age 60. Officers who superannuated on or after March 31 this year may re-join if they have not obtained subsequent employment; the court also asked 24 other states and Union Territories to reconsider enhancing retirement age within two weeks and submit compliance reports.

The Supreme Court directed seven consenting states—Chhattisgarh, Karnataka, Madhya Pradesh, Maharashtra, Sikkim, Tamil Nadu and West Bengal—to amend service rules and raise judicial officers' retirement age from 60 to 62 years, preferably within two months, subject to high court suitability assessment at age 60. #### 'Cops assaulting protesters distressing'

**In short:** Supreme Court judge Justice Ujjal Bhuyan said police personnel assaulting protesting students and demonstrators at Jantar Mantar was "distressing" and "a matter of grave concern" while speaking at the launch of "Policing the Republic" by former secretary (security) Yashovardhan Azad in New Delhi. #### SC backs NCTE's teacher appraisal directive

**In short:** The Supreme Court upheld an NCTE public notice requiring Teacher Education Institutions to submit annual Performance Appraisal Reports, setting aside a March 2023 Delhi High Court order that had struck down the September 22, 2019 notice as arbitrary. Justices P S Narasimha and Alok Aradhe held the NCTE Executive Committee Member Secretary was fully empowered to issue the directive and expanded Article 21A duty bearers to include TEIs and the NCTE as the sixth and seventh responsible parties alongside government, local authorities, schools, parents and teachers.

#### TN woman in Gulf gets Embassy help

**In short:** The Indian Embassy in Kuwait has stepped in to assist Vanitha, a Tamil Nadu domestic worker who appealed in a video for help returning home after being tortured and denied payment for months. External Affairs Ministry spokesperson Randhir Jaiswal said the embassy offered assistance as soon as it became aware of the issue and that she is currently at the embassy.

#### Child's account puts man in jail

**In short:** A Delhi court convicted Bharat for murdering Aarti in 2022, citing the "vivid and graphic" eyewitness account of the victim's six-year-old son. Additional Sessions Judge Syed Zishan Ali Warsi found the accused guilty of stabbing Aarti to death based on the child's testimony.

#### India, Belgium should work on lab-grown diamonds: Minister

**In short:** Union Commerce Minister Piyush Goyal on Friday suggested India and Belgium collaborate on lab-grown diamonds' technology and certification, speaking at a high-level India-EU FTA dialogue with visiting Belgian Prime Minister Bart De Wever in Mumbai. Goyal said the initiative could be anchored in Antwerp and in Mumbai and Surat, while Wever inaugurated HRD Antwerp's expanded facility at the Bharat Diamond Bourse and argued for zero tariffs on diamonds under the January India-EU FTA.

#### Keralam BJP picks 'Mission 2029' in-charge for polls

**In short:** Keralam BJP president Rajeev Chandrasekhar on Friday announced organisational changes for the 2029 Lok Sabha elections, appointing general secretary Anoop Antony—close to Prime Minister Narendra Modi—as "Mission 2029" narrative in-charge and state-cell in-charge, dividing 14 districts into two clusters under M T Ramesh and S Suresh, and naming vice-president Shone George in charge of Minority Morcha, state media and social media. Party sources said the focus is bringing more youngsters to leadership roles, and giving key roles to Anoop Antony and Shone George is seen as wooing the Christian community after their strong Assembly poll performances in Christian belts.

#### CPI(ML)L to launch campaign against Centre

**In short:** The CPI(ML) Liberation is launching a "School Theek Karo-Sikhsha Sudharo" campaign from Sunday, capitalising on "Gen Z anger" against the Modi government, with at least eight programmes planned in Uttar Pradesh this month. Party general secretary Dipankar Bhattacharya and AISA president Neha Bora—who sat on an indefinite hunger strike in July—will participate in a Patna programme on Sunday, with the phase expected to culminate on October 5 at Kolkata's Jadavpur University; the party has already held related programmes in Keralam, West Bengal, Jharkhand, Karnataka, Telangana and Maharashtra.

#### Attack on democracy: Cong on blast outside Manipur leader's house

**In short:** Manipur Congress on Friday condemned a bomb blast outside legislature party leader Keisham Meghachandra's Thoubal residence on Thursday evening as an "attack on democracy," with spokesperson Hareshwar Goswami saying differences should be resolved through dialogue. Goswami said the party had received no information on the motive and alleged miscreants "who may have been instigated by someone" were responsible; police said unidentified men triggered the blast near Meghachandra's gate.

#### Bombay HC slams Maha over manual scavenging

**In short:** The Bombay High Court quashed a Maharashtra government policy shifting compensation for sewer deaths onto private societies and employers, directing the state to immediately pay Rs 30,00,000 to dependants of each person who died in hazardous cleaning and recover amounts from responsible private entities later. Justices Bharati Dangre and Manjusha Deshpande, ruling on a Shramik Janata Sangh petition against December 12, 2019 and April 30, 2025 government resolutions, ordered identification within six months of all such deaths under the 2013 Manual Scavengers Act and lamented that caste-driven manual scavenging persists despite India reaching the moon.

#### 'Terrorist' killed in J&K encounter

**In short:** Security forces killed an unidentified terrorist in Budgam's Ashdar Gali on Friday during Operation 'Ashdar', the second confirmed gunfight in the Kashmir Valley this year, after acting on intelligence about insurgent movement from Pakherpora in Pulwama towards Yousmarg. The Army's Chinar Corps said troops observed suspicious activity, were fired upon when challenged, and recovered one AK rifle and war-like stores; the operation in the rugged Pir Panjal terrain follows a relative lull since Lashkar-e-Taiba terrorist Zakir Ahmad Ganie was killed in Shopian in July.

#### HarperCollins India to bring out Sonia Gandhi's memoir

**In short:** HarperCollins India on Friday announced it will publish Congress leader Sonia Gandhi's memoir Belonging: A Journey of Love in India on November 10, the same date as its international release by Alfred A. Knopf, ending uncertainty after Penguin Random House India withdrew following differences over the manuscript.

HarperCollins India on Friday announced it will publish Congress leader Sonia Gandhi's memoir Belonging: A Journey of Love in India on November 10, the same date as its international release by Alfred A. Knopf, ending uncertainty after Penguin Random House India withdrew following differences over the manuscript. Congress leaders Ashok Gehlot and Kamal Nath had accused Penguin of bowing to government pressure, with Mr. Gehlot alleging contention over sections on Prime Minister Narendra Modi, the RSS and China's occupation of Indian territory; Penguin denied external pressure.

#### Court allows couple in 50s to pursue IVF after son's death

**In short:** The Gujarat High Court on September 2 allowed a couple in their 50s to access IVF after a doctor refused treatment citing age limits, permitting them to avail Assisted Reproductive Technology services. The couple, whose 25-year-old son died by suicide in 2022 while pursuing medical studies, had two children and sought another child after the mother attained menopause and could not conceive naturally; Justice Nirzar Desai allowed their petition.

#### Women journos want digital proof of 'assault'

**In short:** Journalists Shaheen Khan and Nafeesa Khan said on Friday they would move the Delhi High Court to demand CCTV footage of an alleged assault at a south Delhi police station, making the demand at a press conference with their digital news channel editor Sanjay Sharma, who also plans to approach the court. Police rejected the allegations, saying the two women obstructed a designated VVIP route by parking their scooter and refused repeated requests to comply.

#### Akasa Air begins Vietnam operations

**In short:** Akasa Air on Friday commenced operations connecting Mumbai with Hanoi, Vietnam, with four weekly direct flights from Chhatrapati Shivaji Maharaj International Airport to Noi Bai International Airport. The route marks the Indian carrier's seventh international destination and expands its Southeast Asia presence on a high-demand travel corridor.

#### RBI absorbs Rs 6.02 L cr; banking system liquidity surplus hits high

**In short:** The Reserve Bank of India on Friday absorbed over Rs 6.02 lakh crore from the banking system through two variable rate reverse repo (VRRR) auctions as surplus liquidity remained at a record high. The central bank accepted bids worth Rs 5,41,975 crore in a three-day VRRR auction against a notified Rs 7 lakh crore at a cut-off rate of 5.24 per cent, and Rs 60,419 crore in another three-day auction against Rs 1.5 lakh crore, also at 5.24 per cent.

The Reserve Bank of India on Friday absorbed over Rs 6.02 lakh crore from the banking system through two variable rate reverse repo (VRRR) auctions as surplus liquidity remained at a record high. The central bank accepted bids worth Rs 5,41,975 crore in a three-day VRRR auction against a notified Rs 7 lakh crore at a cut-off rate of 5.24 per cent, and Rs 60,419 crore in another three-day auction against Rs 1.5 lakh crore, also at 5.24 per cent. Liquidity surplus is estimated at around Rs 10.32 lakh crore as on September 3, following large inflows through the special FCNR(B) deposit scheme that mobilised USD 136.38 billion by August 31.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** RBI absorbs Rs 6.02 L cr; banking system liquidity surplus hits high
- **Told:** The Reserve Bank of India on Friday absorbed over Rs 6.02 lakh crore from the banking system through two variable rate reverse repo (VRRR) auctions as surplus liquidity remained at a record high.
- **Purpose:** Liquidity surplus is estimated at around Rs 10.32 lakh crore as on September 3, following large inflows through the special FCNR(B) deposit scheme that mobilised USD 136.38 billion by August 31.

**Important points**
- Liquidity surplus is estimated at around Rs 10.32 lakh crore as on September 3, following large inflows through the special FCNR(B) deposit scheme that mobilised USD 136.38 billion by August 31.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.11; News Trail Mangaluru p.10; The Hindu Mangaluru p.13_

#### Fin Min notifies norms allowing e-commerce firms to maintain inventory only for exports

**In short:** The Department of Economic Affairs has notified changes in Foreign Direct Investment norms to allow e-commerce firms to maintain inventory exclusively for export purposes, requiring them to export goods manufactured or produced in India under the Foreign Trade Policy 2023 and related regulations. The September 2 notification adds the provision to the Foreign Exchange Management (Non-debt Instruments) Rules, 2019, bringing into force a decision announced in July; FDI in inventory-based e-commerce retailing for domestic sales remains not permitted.

The Department of Economic Affairs has notified changes in Foreign Direct Investment norms to allow e-commerce firms to maintain inventory exclusively for export purposes, requiring them to export goods manufactured or produced in India under the Foreign Trade Policy 2023 and related regulations. The September 2 notification adds the provision to the Foreign Exchange Management (Non-debt Instruments) Rules, 2019, bringing into force a decision announced in July; FDI in inventory-based e-commerce retailing for domestic sales remains not permitted. Experts said the move would boost traders in small towns and help artisans in handlooms, handicrafts and jewellery access global markets.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Fin Min notifies norms allowing e-commerce firms to maintain inventory only for exports
- **Told:** The Department of Economic Affairs has notified changes in Foreign Direct Investment norms to allow e-commerce firms to maintain inventory exclusively for export purposes, requiring them to export goods manufactured or produced in India under the Foreign Trade Policy 2023 and related regulations.
- **Purpose:** Experts said the move would boost traders in small towns and help artisans in handlooms, handicrafts and jewellery access global markets.

**Important points**

_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.11; News Trail Mangaluru p.10_

#### NSE gets SEBI nod for ₹30,000-crore IPO

**In short:** The National Stock Exchange of India has received SEBI's letter of observation clearing its Draft Red Herring Prospectus for a ₹30,000-crore initial public offering, potentially making it India's second-largest IPO after the proposed Jio Platforms listing. NSE filed the DRHP on June 17 after nearly a decade of delay; the offer will be entirely an offer for sale of up to 14.89 crore equity shares, about 6% of paid-up capital, with no fresh issue.

The National Stock Exchange of India has received SEBI's letter of observation clearing its Draft Red Herring Prospectus for a ₹30,000-crore initial public offering, potentially making it India's second-largest IPO after the proposed Jio Platforms listing. NSE filed the DRHP on June 17 after nearly a decade of delay; the offer will be entirely an offer for sale of up to 14.89 crore equity shares, about 6% of paid-up capital, with no fresh issue. The SBI Group is expected to be the largest seller, offering up to 2.475 crore shares, alongside other major shareholders including MS Strategic (Mauritius) Ltd. and Canada Pension Plan Investment Board.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** NSE gets SEBI nod for ₹30,000-crore IPO
- **Told:** The National Stock Exchange of India has received SEBI's letter of observation clearing its Draft Red Herring Prospectus for a ₹30,000-crore initial public offering, potentially making it India's second-largest IPO after the proposed Jio Platforms listing.
- **Purpose:** and Canada Pension Plan Investment Board.

**Important points**
- The SBI Group is expected to be the largest seller, offering up to 2.475 crore shares, alongside other major shareholders including MS Strategic (Mauritius) Ltd.
- and Canada Pension Plan Investment Board.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.11; The Hindu Mangaluru p.13_

#### Adobe names Anil Chakravarthy as new president and CEO

**In short:** Adobe on Thursday named Anil Chakravarthy, president of its Customer Experience Orchestration business and worldwide field operations, as President and CEO effective December 1, succeeding Shantanu Narayen who transitions to Executive Chair after more than 18 years. An IIT (BHU) Varanasi and MIT alumnus who studied in Basavanagudi, Bengaluru, during 1983–85 and previously served four years as Informatica's CEO, Chakravarthy was chosen as David Wadhwani, president of Adobe's Creativity and Productivity Business, announced his departure.

#### Forex kitty jumps to all-time high of $740.8 bn

**In short:** India's forex reserves jumped USD 11.475 billion to a new all-time high of USD 740.803 billion during the week ended August 28, the RBI said on Friday, up from USD 729.328 billion the previous week. The prior record of USD 728.494 billion was set in the week ended February 27, before Middle East conflict led to weeks of decline as the RBI intervened through dollar sales; reserves have since risen after concessional forex swap initiatives announced in June amid rupee depreciation, yielding over USD 132 billion in new flows.

India's forex reserves jumped USD 11.475 billion to a new all-time high of USD 740.803 billion during the week ended August 28, the RBI said on Friday, up from USD 729.328 billion the previous week. The prior record of USD 728.494 billion was set in the week ended February 27, before Middle East conflict led to weeks of decline as the RBI intervened through dollar sales; reserves have since risen after concessional forex swap initiatives announced in June amid rupee depreciation, yielding over USD 132 billion in new flows. Foreign currency assets, a major component of the reserves, increased by USD 9.337 billion during the week.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Forex kitty jumps to all-time high of $740.8 bn
- **Told:** India's forex reserves jumped USD 11.475 billion to a new all-time high of USD 740.803 billion during the week ended August 28, the RBI said on Friday, up from USD 729.328 billion the previous week.
- **Purpose:** Foreign currency assets, a major component of the reserves, increased by USD 9.337 billion during the week.

**Important points**
- Foreign currency assets, a major component of the reserves, increased by USD 9.337 billion during the week.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.11; News Trail Mangaluru p.10_

#### Chasing the ghost of the HIMALAYAS

**In short:** Conservationist Kulbhushansingh Suryawanshi, who has tracked snow leopards in high-altitude plateaus for two decades, recounts spotting his first wild snow leopard in Spiti Valley on January 27, 2007, and later leading the women-led Shen handicraft enterprise and India Programme of the Snow Leopard Trust. He warns that downgrading the IUCN status without adequate data, solar parks fragmenting habitat, and compensation-only approaches ignore villagers' emotional ties to livestock; his book 'The Ghost of the Mountains' is out now from Penguin Random House.

Conservationist Kulbhushansingh Suryawanshi, who has tracked snow leopards in high-altitude plateaus for two decades, recounts spotting his first wild snow leopard in Spiti Valley on January 27, 2007, and later leading the women-led Shen handicraft enterprise and India Programme of the Snow Leopard Trust. He warns that downgrading the IUCN status without adequate data, solar parks fragmenting habitat, and compensation-only approaches ignore villagers' emotional ties to livestock; his book 'The Ghost of the Mountains' is out now from Penguin Random House. India is estimated to have about 700 snow leopards, with roughly two-thirds in Ladakh, while the global population is put at 3,500 to 7,000.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Chasing the ghost of the HIMALAYAS
- **Told:** Conservationist Kulbhushansingh Suryawanshi, who has tracked snow leopards in high-altitude plateaus for two decades, recounts spotting his first wild snow leopard in Spiti Valley on January 27, 2007, and later leading the women-led Shen handicraft enterprise and India Programme of the Snow Leopard Trust.
- **Purpose:** India is estimated to have about 700 snow leopards, with roughly two-thirds in Ladakh, while the global population is put at 3,500 to 7,000.

**Important points**
- India is estimated to have about 700 snow leopards, with roughly two-thirds in Ladakh, while the global population is put at 3,500 to 7,000.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.13_

#### Mirzapur: The Movie — Cult franchise, enjoyable on big screen

**In short:** The theatrical 'Mirzapur' film, directed by Gurmeet Singh, brings Kaleen Bhaiyya (Pankaj Tripathi), Guddu (Ali Fazal), Bablu (Jitendra Kumar) and rival drug lord Babban Yadav (Ravi Kishan) to the big screen with taut direction, whip-smart dialogues and gory violence. As Babban closes in on Kaleen in the second half, loyalty is tested in a blood-curdling climax; Pankaj Tripathi, Ravi Kishan, Jitendra Kumar, Divyendu Sharma and Sheeba Chadda deliver outstanding performances.

#### 20% rise in Indian cancer patients in S'pore

**In short:** A Singapore-based cancer treatment centre has seen a nearly 20 per cent rise in patients from India in 2025, with a majority seeking care at an advanced stage when fewer curative options remain, according to Parkway Cancer Centre medical director Dr Ang Peng Tiam. Many patients travel abroad after exhausting available treatment options in India.

#### Delhi cops allegedly assault 2 Muslim journos

**In short:** Two women journalists, Shaheen and Nafisa Khan, were allegedly detained and assaulted by Delhi Police on Thursday while covering a private hospital event attended by Union Home Minister Amit Shah and Delhi Chief Minister Rekha Gupta; Shaheen alleged they were beaten with batons for posing questions and because they are Muslims. The Press Council of India, Indian Women's Press Corps, Delhi Union of Journalists, Congress and Samajwadi Party condemned the incident, including alleged religion-specific abuses at Saket police station.

- Two women journalists, Shaheen and Nafisa Khan, were allegedly detained and assaulted by Delhi Police on Thursday while covering a private hospital event attended by Union Home Minister Amit Shah and Delhi Chief Minister Rekha Gupta; Shaheen alleged they were beaten with batons for posing questions and because they are Muslims.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.1_

#### 'Hatemonger' who bragged about cracking man's skull detained after CJP protest

**In short:** Delhi Police detained Hindutva influencer Swatantra Bhardwaj in Uttar Pradesh on Friday, hours after the Cockroach Janta Party protested outside Parliament Street police station demanding his arrest for assaulting Dalit victim Sanjay Kumar's father — a crime Bhardwaj bragged about in an interview, claiming he cracked the skull, the man needed 60 stitches, and he was not jailed due to political connections. CJP representatives with Azad Samaj Party chief Chandrashekhar Azad and MP Pappu Yadav were assured action within 72 hours and sought attempt-to-murder, criminal intimidation and SC/ST Act charges plus a separate FIR over alleged rape threats and abuse targeting Kumar's daughter Nishu.

#### BEML, UMPESL forge partnership to drive next-generation mining fleet productivity

**In short:** Leading defence PSU BEML has signed a strategic MoU with Universal MEP Projects and Engineering Services Limited (UMPESL), a wholly owned Voltas subsidiary, to jointly pursue productivity-driven Fleet Management Contracts across India's coal-fields. Signed at BEML Headquarters in Bengaluru, the partnership combines BEML's OEM expertise in heavy mining equipment with UMPESL's field service and fleet management capabilities, focusing on O&M projects for Coal India Limited and its subsidiaries covering bulldozers, excavators, loaders, dumpers and surface miners.

#### How is demand for good schools anti-govt narrative, asks Dipke

**In short:** Cockroach Janta Party founder Abhijeet Dipke has asked how a demand for good schools or improvement in their condition can be termed an anti-government narrative, urging Maharashtra Chief Minister Devendra Fadnavis to visit 'ashramshalas' for a "reality check" after meeting tribal students protesting in Nashik. More than 100 tribal students from different parts of Maharashtra are participating in the protest at Eidgah ground in Nashik, with three on hunger strike over demands including better food and students' safety; Dipke said the disparity in facilities, food and education provided to tribal students was "completely unacceptable" and criticised what he called the government's policy of privatising schools and educational facilities.

#### Naidu asks TDP cadre to gear up for local body polls

**In short:** Andhra Pradesh Chief Minister N Chandrababu Naidu on Thursday directed the TDP machinery to gear up for the upcoming local body elections and aim for a better strike rate than in the 2024 Assembly polls, while stressing coordination among NDA partners. At a meeting with ministers and zonal observers, Naidu said the local body elections would provide an opportunity to assess the party's current position compared to the 2024 polls and noted this would be the first time the TDP would face local elections as part of an alliance, adding that there should be no gap between alliance parties under any circumstances.

#### Release Nagarjuna Sagar water, warns KTR

**In short:** BRS Working President K T Rama Rao said he would undertake a padayatra from Kodad to Haliya with thousands of farmers if the Telangana government failed to announce a schedule for releasing water into the Nagarjuna Sagar Left Canal by September 14. Alleging that the Congress government was withholding water from the Left Canal despite around 180 TMC being available in the Nagarjuna Sagar project, KTR said farmers were anxious about their crops and demanded a clear water-release schedule by September 14.

#### Temples of Goa were destroyed by Portuguese: CM

**In short:** Goa would have had temples comparable to those in south India had the Portuguese not arrived in the coastal state, Chief Minister Pramod Sawant said on Friday while announcing the restoration of the historic Koti Tirth site in Divar. Sawant said several temples were destroyed during Portuguese rule and the Koti Tirth Corridor project, envisioned as a heritage initiative representing temples and religious sites destroyed during the Portuguese period, would be completed within a year; he also said the ancient Gopakapattana Port in St Andre constituency would be developed in the future.
