#### 37 evacuated as bus catches fire

**In short:** All 37 people aboard a Bengaluru-Hyderabad private bus escaped unhurt after it caught fire near Miduturu village in Anantapur district in the early hours of Friday, police said. The fire broke out at around 2.50 am at the rear of the vehicle due to unknown causes; a passenger seated at the back spotted the flames and alerted the driver, who stopped the bus immediately, allowing all 33 passengers and four staff members to get off safely before the fire engulfed the vehicle.

All 37 people aboard a Bengaluru-Hyderabad private bus escaped unhurt after it caught fire near Miduturu village in Anantapur district in the early hours of Friday, police said. The fire broke out at around 2.50 am at the rear of the vehicle due to unknown causes; a passenger seated at the back spotted the flames and alerted the driver, who stopped the bus immediately, allowing all 33 passengers and four staff members to get off safely before the fire engulfed the vehicle. Fire tenders brought the blaze under control and police have registered a case of accidental fire, with the exact cause yet to be ascertained.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** 37 evacuated as bus catches fire

#### Maha govt must frame policy on loud DJs: Raut

**In short:** Shiv Sena (UBT) leader Sanjay Raut on Friday said the Maharashtra government must formulate a policy on the use of high-decibel DJ sound systems and laser lights during festivals, alleging that Chief Minister Devendra Fadnavis lacks the courage to act on the issue. Criticising the state government following the attack on activist Vidyanand Bapat, who was assaulted on Thursday in Pune while campaigning against noise pollution during Ganeshotsav, Raut said isolated bans in cities were inadequate as the issue concerns public health; the Nagpur police on Thursday issued an order banning high-powered and modified sound systems as well as beams and laser lights during the forthcoming festival season.

#### 9 newborns evacuated as fire erupts in NICU of private hospital

**In short:** Nine newborns were shifted after a fire broke out in the NICU of Asian Hospital in Chhatrapati Sambhajinagar, Maharashtra, in the early hours of Friday, the hospital administration said. Dr Shoeb Hashmi said staff noticed smoke coming out of an AC unit in the NICU and, as a precaution, the team evacuated the infants along with patients in the neighbouring general ward; the newborns were shifted to other hospitals with family consent, no injuries were reported, and the hospital has been cleaned and is functioning as per routine.

Nine newborns were shifted after a fire broke out in the NICU of Asian Hospital in Chhatrapati Sambhajinagar, Maharashtra, in the early hours of Friday, the hospital administration said. Dr Shoeb Hashmi said staff noticed smoke coming out of an AC unit in the NICU and, as a precaution, the team evacuated the infants along with patients in the neighbouring general ward; the newborns were shifted to other hospitals with family consent, no injuries were reported, and the hospital has been cleaned and is functioning as per routine. About ten days ago, three newborns died and 36 were rescued after a fire in the NICU of the District Women's Hospital in Amravati, prompting a high-level state inquiry.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** 9 newborns evacuated as fire erupts in NICU of private hospital
- **Told:** Nine newborns were shifted after a fire broke out in the NICU of Asian Hospital in Chhatrapati Sambhajinagar, Maharashtra, in the early hours of Friday, the hospital administration said.
- **Purpose:** About ten days ago, three newborns died and 36 were rescued after a fire in the NICU of the District Women's Hospital in Amravati, prompting a high-level state inquiry.

#### Bandi Sanjay slams Telangana CM over 'patriotism' remarks on Owaisi brothers

**In short:** Union Minister of State for Home Bandi Sanjay Kumar on Friday hit out at Telangana Chief Minister A Revanth Reddy over his remarks that AIMIM president Asaduddin Owaisi and his brother Akbaruddin Owaisi were as patriotic as Prime Minister Narendra Modi and Union Home Minister Amit Shah. Bandi Sanjay questioned whether AIMIM's alleged opposition to the full version of Vande Mataram, the abrogation of Article 370 and the Citizenship Amendment Act amounted to patriotism, cited a controversial 2013 speech by Akbaruddin Owaisi and alleged terror links involving institutions connected to the Owaisi family, asking whether the CM should first clarify to Congress leader Rahul Gandhi, who had accused AIMIM of helping the BJP, before issuing "certificates" of patriotism.

#### Brahmin activist ends hunger strike

**In short:** Brahmin community activist Deepak Rannaware ended his hunger strike in Jalna on Friday following the intervention of state BJP spokesperson Ram Kulkarni. Rannaware had been on a hunger strike at Gandhi Chaman for the past six days seeking resolution of various demands concerning the Brahmin community; Kulkarni visited the protest site, apprised Chief Minister Devendra Fadnavis and Jalna district guardian minister Pankaja Munde about the demands, and it was decided that a meeting between representatives of the Brahmin community and Fadnavis would be held in Mumbai soon.

#### How India shaped the feminist vision of Gloria Steinem

**In short:** The passing of renowned American feminist pioneer Gloria Steinem on Thursday at the age of 92 has triggered tributes highlighting the deep, lifelong connection she maintained with India. A 22-year-old Steinem arrived in India in 1956 on a Chester Bowles Asian Fellowship, spending nearly two years in the country including about a year at Miranda House hostel in Delhi, travelling alone in third-class women-only railway carriages, walking with Gandhian activists in Ramnad, Tamil Nadu, and participating in Vinoba Bhave's Bhoodan land-reform movement; the collective listening practice of village meetings under kerosene lamps became her most enduring organizational tool, later translated into the consciousness-raising groups that drove the American women's movement, and she formed lifelong bonds with Indian feminists including Devaki Jain, Ela Bhatt, Ruchira Gupta and Kamaladevi Chattopadhyay.

**What this is**

#### Needle found in baby's body; medical neglect alleged

**In short:** A needle was found lodged inside a one-year-old girl's body, with her parents alleging on Friday that it may have been left behind during treatment at Kottayam Medical College Hospital. The needle was detected near the child's right ribs during examination at Kozhikode Medical College Hospital in July 2026 and surgically removed; the child, daughter of Anju and Vineesh of Chathamangalam in Kozhikode district, was born at Kottayam Medical College Hospital in June 2025 and shifted to the NICU after developing jaundice, and Anju said doctors dismissed a swelling near her right ribs as a minor growth that would subside, until scans at Kozhikode Medical College Hospital in July 2026 revealed the needle, which doctors said appeared to have been inside the body for a considerable period.

#### TN woman in Kuwait alleges abuse

**In short:** A domestic worker from Kallakurichi district has sent an SOS video from Kuwait, alleging torture, starvation and non-payment of wages by her employers and appealing to Indian authorities to rescue her. The woman, who said she travelled to Kuwait three months ago through an employment agent, alleged she had been denied proper food for more than 50 days and not paid her salary for the past two months, requesting Collector J E Padmaja and SP Shahnaz I to rescue her alive; her mother Vennila appealed to the district administration, alleging an employment agent had misled the family about job conditions.

**In short:** An IndiGo flight operating from Navi Mumbai to Srinagar collided with a parking stand pole during the parking process at Srinagar International Airport on Friday. Flight 6E 225 came into contact with a metal pole while being positioned at Bay No.

An IndiGo flight operating from Navi Mumbai to Srinagar collided with a parking stand pole during the parking process at Srinagar International Airport on Friday. #### End attacks on TMC, Mamata warns BJP

**In short:** TMC supremo Mamata Banerjee on Thursday turned the alleged attack on her party's Camac Street office into a direct political challenge to the ruling BJP, warning that she would travel to New Delhi and stage dharnas outside the residences of its leaders if "atrocities" against the Trinamool continued. In a combative Facebook Live, Banerjee accused the BJP of sending "goons" to vandalise the TMC office in the early hours, assault party workers and loot documents and electronic equipment; TMC national general secretary Abhishek Banerjee alleged armed men backed by the BJP stormed the office around 4.30 am, and police have arrested six people in connection with the incident.

#### SC dismisses NEET-PG 2026 re-test plea

**In short:** The Supreme Court has dismissed a Public Interest Litigation seeking a fresh conduct of the NEET-PG 2026 examination, severely admonishing the petitioner and counsel for misusing judicial forums and creating nationwide panic. A bench comprising Justices PS Narasimha and Alok Aradhe threw out the petition, levying a penalty of ₹25,000 on the petitioner and warning it would refer the petitioner's advocate to the Bar Council of India for disciplinary action regarding social media solicitation; NBEMS clarified that out of 1,111 centres across India, administrative power outages were limited to only two centres at the iON Digital Zone in Jaipur affecting around 2,500 candidates, and the petitioners from Noida and Punjab were entirely unaffected and not even appearing in the exam themselves.

#### Row over women's role: Islamic scholar urges followers not to be 'provoked'

**In short:** Islamic scholar Kanthapuram A P Aboobacker Musliyar, facing criticism over his alleged anti-women remarks, on Friday urged his followers not to be provoked or allow "spiritual discussions" to be diverted into controversies and divisions. In a Facebook post, the veteran Sunni scholar said discussions should provide healthy platforms to objectively assess ideas and learn new things, alleged that vested interests deliberately try to divert spiritual discussions towards controversies and divisions, and urged followers to respond to any provocation with maturity and wisdom rather than emotion, noting that the tradition of Sunni believers has always been to confront all evils only with goodness.

#### El Nino to get worse; what it means for India

**In short:** The prevailing El Nino conditions over the equatorial Pacific are most likely to intensify into a very strong event in the coming months and persist through February 2027, the World Meteorological Organization has said, warning of substantial impacts on rainfall and temperature patterns and associated risks of floods, drought and extreme heat. The IMD confirmed that overall rainfall in India during August was deficient at 16 per cent due to prevailing El Nino conditions, with August being the hottest since 1901, and has forecast September rainfall to be below 91 per cent of the long period average; an intensifying El Nino means higher temperatures during the winter season, with a Climate Trends study finding February-March warming in northern India is increasing the risk of terminal heat stress on Rabi crops like wheat during reproductive and grain filling stages.

**What this is**

**Important points**

#### Ola Electric's first dealer stores are now open across India

**In short:** Ola Electric on Friday launched its first cohort of network-partner stores, with dealer-operated outlets going live in Rajasthan, Tamil Nadu, Maharashtra, Bihar, Telangana, Uttar Pradesh and Madhya Pradesh. The stores deepen the company's regional presence and will serve local demand for Ola scooters and motorcycles, marking a structural shift five years after Ola opened its first scooter through a fully company-owned model.

#### Kia to follow multi-tech pathway for mobility: MD

**In short:** Kia India will follow a multi-technology pathway including hybrids, CNG and electric vehicles in its drive for sustainable mobility, MD and CEO Gwang Gu Lee said on Friday. The company, which launched its premium seven-seater SUV Sorento in strong hybrid and diesel variants priced between Rs 27.99 lakh and Rs 40.39 lakh, believes hybrids will be a key part of its portfolio as different global markets take different routes—strong hybrid demand in the US, more EV traction in the EU and China.

Lee said it is hard to focus on one technology alone in India, which is why Kia presented multiple power trains.

#### Tata Motors' Iveco takeover gets Consob nod

**In short:** Tata Motors said Italy's market regulator Consob has approved the offer document for its voluntary tender offer to acquire all common shares of Iveco Group NV. The acceptance period for Iveco shareholders will begin on September 7 and close on October 26 unless extended, with Tata Motors paying Euro 14.10 per common share including dividend; TML CV Holdings BV received Consob approval on September 3, 2026.

Tata Motors said Italy's market regulator Consob has approved the offer document for its voluntary tender offer to acquire all common shares of Iveco Group NV. The acceptance period for Iveco shareholders will begin on September 7 and close on October 26 unless extended, with Tata Motors paying Euro 14.10 per common share including dividend; TML CV Holdings BV received Consob approval on September 3, 2026. Shareholders who tender during the acceptance period will be paid on October 30, 2026 unless extended, with a possible five-day reopening from November 2 to November 6 and payment on November 13 if legal conditions are met.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Tata Motors' Iveco takeover gets Consob nod
- **Told:** Tata Motors said Italy's market regulator Consob has approved the offer document for its voluntary tender offer to acquire all common shares of Iveco Group NV.

**Important points**
- Shareholders who tender during the acceptance period will be paid on October 30, 2026 unless extended, with a possible five-day reopening from November 2 to November 6 and payment on November 13 if legal conditions are met.

#### Govt's Rs 37k cr coal gasification fails to attract bids

**In short:** The government's Rs 37,500-crore financial incentive scheme for promoting coal gasification has found no taker so far despite a September 7 deadline, with no application from any private or public player to set up projects under the programme, sources said. The Union Cabinet-approved scheme aims to gasify 75 million tonnes of coal and lignite, boost clean energy production and reduce dependence on imports of LNG, urea and methanol.

The government's Rs 37,500-crore financial incentive scheme for promoting coal gasification has found no taker so far despite a September 7 deadline, with no application from any private or public player to set up projects under the programme, sources said. The Union Cabinet-approved scheme aims to gasify 75 million tonnes of coal and lignite, boost clean energy production and reduce dependence on imports of LNG, urea and methanol. Coal gasification converts dry fuel into synthetic gas used as alternative fuel to support production of methanol, fertilisers, hydrogen and chemicals while cutting carbon emissions and import reliance.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Govt's Rs 37k cr coal gasification fails to attract bids
- **Told:** The government's Rs 37,500-crore financial incentive scheme for promoting coal gasification has found no taker so far despite a September 7 deadline, with no application from any private or public player to set up projects under the programme, sources said.

#### Govt urges industry to lead Rs 60K cr PM-SETU initiative

**In short:** The government has urged industry to take a leading role in transforming the country's skilling landscape through the Rs 60,000 crore PM-SETU initiative to drive India's energy and industrial transformation. MSDE Secretary Debashree Mukherjee, speaking on the concluding day of Bharat Electricity 2026 on Thursday, said industry partners will hold a controlling 51 per cent stake in Section 8 companies managing ITI clusters, with government and states providing bulk funding and industry's 17 per cent share eligible under CSR.

The government has urged industry to take a leading role in transforming the country's skilling landscape through the Rs 60,000 crore PM-SETU initiative to drive India's energy and industrial transformation. MSDE Secretary Debashree Mukherjee, speaking on the concluding day of Bharat Electricity 2026 on Thursday, said industry partners will hold a controlling 51 per cent stake in Section 8 companies managing ITI clusters, with government and states providing bulk funding and industry's 17 per cent share eligible under CSR. She cited Arcelor-Mittal's leadership in the Vizag cluster as a benchmark and called on industry to move from recruiters to co-creators in curriculum development, trainer investment and infrastructure.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Govt urges industry to lead Rs 60K cr PM-SETU initiative
- **Told:** The government has urged industry to take a leading role in transforming the country's skilling landscape through the Rs 60,000 crore PM-SETU initiative to drive India's energy and industrial transformation.

#### Adani Ports to launch dedicated container yard

**In short:** Adani Ports and Special Economic Zone Ltd (APSEZ) will start dedicated empty container yard operations at its Mundra Port and SEZ to streamline container handling and improve service for shipping lines and customers. #### Sensex rebounds 362 pts, snaps 4-day losses

**In short:** Benchmark BSE Sensex rebounded 362.57 points, or 0.48 per cent, to settle at 76,515.43 on Friday, snapping a four-day losing streak on buying in metal, private banking and oil and gas shares amid gains in global stock markets, with 20 of 30 constituents ending higher. #### Charges added to FIR on attacker after CJP stir

**In short:** After the Cockroach Janta Party (CJP) protest, CJP co-convenor Saurav Das said Sanjay Kumar's skull was "cracked open" and the wound warranted an attempt-to-murder charge in the FIR; he also alleged that others accompanying Swatantra Bhardwaj were not identified and that some had posted videos threatening protester Nishu Aazad. Ms.

After the Cockroach Janta Party (CJP) protest, CJP co-convenor Saurav Das said Sanjay Kumar's skull was "cracked open" and the wound warranted an attempt-to-murder charge in the FIR; he also alleged that others accompanying Swatantra Bhardwaj were not identified and that some had posted videos threatening protester Nishu Aazad. Ms. Aazad said she faced rape threats, abuse and offensive stickers online, lodged a separate complaint with an FIR registered, and could not go to school for two to four weeks after the incident. New Delhi DCP Sachin Sharma disputed the CJP account, saying Mr. Sanjay sustained a simple head injury from a kada during a scuffle at Ram Manohar Lohia Hospital, and that medical examination did not support claims of a skull crack or weapon use.

- **Purpose:** Sanjay sustained a simple head injury from a kada during a scuffle at Ram Manohar Lohia Hospital, and that medical examination did not support claims of a skull crack or weapon use.

#### Panel meet fails to arrive at consensus on water sharing

**In short:** A three-member Krishna River Management Board committee meeting in Hyderabad on Friday failed to agree on the water-sharing proportion for 2026-27 amid El Nino conditions, as Andhra Pradesh insisted on continuing the ad hoc 66:34 (AP:TG) arrangement agreed for 2015-16 while Telangana stood firm for a 50% share since 2020. After deadlock in two successive meetings, another session was scheduled for the fourth week of September, and officials said a full-fledged meeting with State Secretaries and the board Chairman is likely in the last week of this month; as on September 4, Srisailam had received 213.6 tmc ft inflows since June 1 while Nagarjunasagar received 93.1 tmc ft including releases from Srisailam.

A three-member Krishna River Management Board committee meeting in Hyderabad on Friday failed to agree on the water-sharing proportion for 2026-27 amid El Nino conditions, as Andhra Pradesh insisted on continuing the ad hoc 66:34 (AP:TG) arrangement agreed for 2015-16 while Telangana stood firm for a 50% share since 2020. **What this is**
- **Concept:** Panel meet fails to arrive at consensus on water sharing

#### Don't attack crocodiles, forest staff tell public after viral clip

**In short:** Forest Department officials in Kurnool district, Andhra Pradesh, have warned the public not to harm crocodiles after a viral video showed people trying to beat a crocodile with sticks on the banks of the Tungabhadra at Naguladinne village. The Kurnool District Forest Officer said crocodiles are protected under Schedule-1 of the Wildlife Protection Act, 1972, and officials noted crocodiles are coming out of rivers as water levels have reduced.

#### Keralam govt. finalises terms of reference for Messi visit SIT probe

**In short:** The Congress-led United Democratic Front government has fixed the terms of reference for an SIT probe into allegations of service tax fraud, corruption, suspected money-laundering, abuse of constitutional office and nepotism surrounding the previous LDF government's failed attempt to bring the Argentina football team and Lionel Messi for a friendly at Jawaharlal Nehru Stadium in Kochi in August 2025. Home Minister Ramesh Chennithala has signed off on a remit covering the entire transaction from its inception in 2024, including sponsor selection and information on a U.S.-based entity that allegedly wire-transferred ₹126 crore to the Argentina Football Association in 2025, triggering an unredeemed service tax obligation of ₹22.68 crore to the State exchequer.

#### Missing: boundary walls, bathrooms, blackboards

**In short:** Since August 15, Abhijeet Dipke's Cockroach Janta Party (CJP) "School thik karo (reform schools)" campaign has spotlighted rural government schools through a three-step public audit—download a checklist, conduct the audit, and submit and tag the CJP—covering drinking water, boundary walls, washrooms, and midday meals. In Bihar, Muzaffarpur's Dumri Kewat Tola primary school (119 students, established 1982) has no boundary wall, washroom, sports ground, or midday-meal storage, with children relieving themselves in the open, while nearby Budhnagra middle school crams 283 students into two rooms and Vaishali's Mirpur Pathad school runs 91 students in a single community-centre room; in Rajasthan's Deeg district, students at Rajkiya Prathmik Vidyalayas in Unchki, Amrooka, and Bas Bolkheda walk through graveyards or muddy paths, study in corridors, lack toilets, and see only 50–60% daily attendance.

Since August 15, Abhijeet Dipke's Cockroach Janta Party (CJP) "School thik karo (reform schools)" campaign has spotlighted rural government schools through a three-step public audit—download a checklist, conduct the audit, and submit and tag the CJP—covering drinking water, boundary walls, washrooms, and midday meals. In Bihar, Muzaffarpur's Dumri Kewat Tola primary school (119 students, established 1982) has no boundary wall, washroom, sports ground, or midday-meal storage, with children relieving themselves in the open, while nearby Budhnagra middle school crams 283 students into two rooms and Vaishali's Mirpur Pathad school runs 91 students in a single community-centre room; in Rajasthan's Deeg district, students at Rajkiya Prathmik Vidyalayas in Unchki, Amrooka, and Bas Bolkheda walk through graveyards or muddy paths, study in corridors, lack toilets, and see only 50–60% daily attendance. In Lucknow, cab driver Digvijay Narayan (name changed) plans to move his daughter from Government Primary School Gwari to a private school after a urinary tract infection he blames on a dirty washroom, while in Madhya Pradesh's Sehore district, Hirapur middle school packs 22 students across eight classes into a dilapidated four-room building with one functional student toilet, no benches, and halted midday meals, and Sarvar's outwardly maintained school lacks electricity and adequate repair funds despite ₹25,000 in annual maintenance, as officials say flagship Sandipani and Excellence schools have drawn focus away from struggling rural schools.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Missing: boundary walls, bathrooms, blackboards
- **Told:** Since August 15, Abhijeet Dipke's Cockroach Janta Party (CJP) "School thik karo (reform schools)" campaign has spotlighted rural government schools through a three-step public audit—download a checklist, conduct the audit, and submit and tag the CJP—covering drinking water, boundary walls, washrooms, and midday meals.
- **Purpose:** In Lucknow, cab driver Digvijay Narayan (name changed) plans to move his daughter from Government Primary School Gwari to a private school after a urinary tract infection he blames on a dirty washroom, while in Madhya Pradesh's Sehore district, Hirapur middle school packs 22 students across eight classes into a dilapidated four-room building with one functional student toilet, no benches, and halted midday meals, and Sarvar's outwardly maintained school lacks electricity and adequate repair funds despite ₹25,000 in annual maintenance, as officials say flagship Sandipani and Excellence schools have drawn focus away from struggling rural schools.

**Important points**
- In Lucknow, cab driver Digvijay Narayan (name changed) plans to move his daughter from Government Primary School Gwari to a private school after a urinary tract infection he blames on a dirty washroom, while in Madhya Pradesh's Sehore district.
- Hirapur middle school packs 22 students across eight classes into a dilapidated four-room building with one functional student toilet, no benches, and halted midday meals, and Sarvar's outwardly maintained school lacks electricity and adequate repair funds despite ₹25,000 in annual maintenance, as officials say flagship Sandipani and Excellence schools have drawn focus away from struggling rural schools.

#### CBI searches 89 locations across 20 States in crackdown against 3 'digital arrest' cases

**In short:** The CBI carried out searches at 89 locations across 20 States in three major "digital arrest" scam cases under Operation Chakra-VI, arresting three persons and seizing banking documents, digital devices and other evidence. In one case linked to BITS Pilani, a victim was kept under "digital arrest" for three months and extorted of ₹7.67 crore; in Gujarat, a victim lost ₹19.24 crore over three months; and in Karnataka, a victim paid ₹15.45 crore over one month.

The CBI carried out searches at 89 locations across 20 States in three major "digital arrest" scam cases under Operation Chakra-VI, arresting three persons and seizing banking documents, digital devices and other evidence. In one case linked to BITS Pilani, a victim was kept under "digital arrest" for three months and extorted of ₹7.67 crore; in Gujarat, a victim lost ₹19.24 crore over three months; and in Karnataka, a victim paid ₹15.45 crore over one month. The agency said Akash of Haryana received ₹1.95 crore, Raja Karmakar of Kolkata received ₹1.5 crore in his firm's account, and Jyoti Rani withdrew and transferred portions of the defrauded sum.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** CBI searches 89 locations across 20 States in crackdown against 3 'digital arrest' cases
- **Told:** The CBI carried out searches at 89 locations across 20 States in three major "digital arrest" scam cases under Operation Chakra-VI, arresting three persons and seizing banking documents, digital devices and other evidence.
- **Purpose:** The agency said Akash of Haryana received ₹1.95 crore, Raja Karmakar of Kolkata received ₹1.5 crore in his firm's account, and Jyoti Rani withdrew and transferred portions of the defrauded sum.

**Important points**
- The agency said Akash of Haryana received ₹1.95 crore, Raja Karmakar of Kolkata received ₹1.5 crore in his firm's account, and Jyoti Rani withdrew and transferred portions of the defrauded sum.

#### Air India sacks Delhi-Phuket flight pilot after AAIB report

**In short:** Air India on Friday sacked the pilot-in-command of an Aug. 4 Delhi-Phuket flight after he tested positive for psychoactive substance in a confirmatory test; the flight momentarily lost altitude, injuring 24 passengers.

Air India on Friday sacked the pilot-in-command of an Aug. 4 Delhi-Phuket flight after he tested positive for psychoactive substance in a confirmatory test; the flight momentarily lost altitude, injuring 24 passengers. The Aircraft Accident Investigation Bureau's preliminary report recommended DGCA action on priority, noting all three hydraulic systems failed, the autopilot disengaged and a stall warning sounded for two seconds as the aircraft gained 372 ft and fell 292 ft from flight level 360; a detailed probe into technical aspects is under way. The co-pilot regained control as systems recovered, but with seatbelt signs off at cruising altitude, passengers and crew were injured and the cabin sustained damage including cracked ceiling panels, broken handrails and a dislodged lavatory commode.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Air India sacks Delhi-Phuket flight pilot after AAIB report
- **Told:** Air India on Friday sacked the pilot-in-command of an Aug.
- **Purpose:** The co-pilot regained control as systems recovered, but with seatbelt signs off at cruising altitude, passengers and crew were injured and the cabin sustained damage including cracked ceiling panels, broken handrails and a dislodged lavatory commode.

**Important points**
- The Aircraft Accident Investigation Bureau's preliminary report recommended DGCA action on priority, noting all three hydraulic systems failed, the autopilot disengaged and a stall warning sounded for two seconds as the aircraft gained 372 ft and fell 292 ft from flight level 360.
- a detailed probe into technical aspects is under way.
- The co-pilot regained control as systems recovered, but with seatbelt signs off at cruising altitude, passengers and crew were injured and the cabin sustained damage including cracked ceiling panels, broken handrails and a dislodged lavatory commode.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.10_

#### NCPCR campaign rescues over 3,800 from child labour

**In short:** The National Commission for Protection of Child Rights has rescued over 3,800 children from child labour and registered more than 575 FIRs under a Pan-India Rescue and Rehabilitation campaign launched on June 12 and run until August 31. The statutory body coordinated with State and UT administrations, district magistrates, labour departments and police to identify, rescue and rehabilitate children in labour and those vulnerable to trafficking, and held district-level meetings to review operations and strengthen inter-department coordination.

#### Centre's rationale for not using OBC lists for caste enumeration is flawed, say associations

**In short:** OBC associations said on Friday that the government's argument that OBC lists cannot be used for caste enumeration because they list "classes, not castes" is "fundamentally flawed," a day after The Hindu reported this as part of the Union rationale for not drawing caste names from Central and State OBC lists for the 2027 Census. The All India OBC Students' Association, OBC Public Representatives Forum chair V.

OBC associations said on Friday that the government's argument that OBC lists cannot be used for caste enumeration because they list "classes, not castes" is "fundamentally flawed," a day after The Hindu reported this as part of the Union rationale for not drawing caste names from Central and State OBC lists for the 2027 Census. The All India OBC Students' Association, OBC Public Representatives Forum chair V. Srinivas Goud and Backwards Classes Intellectual Forum chairman T. Chiranjeevulu argued the government should rationalise caste names from existing lists rather than drop them, and that refusing to enumerate OBC populations scientifically could expose overestimation of the EWS-eligible population cited when EWS reservations were introduced; Opposition leaders including Mallikarjun Kharge and Rahul Gandhi have questioned relying on an open-column methodology instead.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Centre's rationale for not using OBC lists for caste enumeration is flawed, say associations
- **Told:** OBC associations said on Friday that the government's argument that OBC lists cannot be used for caste enumeration because they list "classes, not castes" is "fundamentally flawed," a day after The Hindu reported this as part of the Union rationale for not drawing caste names from Central and State OBC lists for the 2027 Census.
- **Purpose:** Chiranjeevulu argued the government should rationalise caste names from existing lists rather than drop them, and that refusing to enumerate OBC populations scientifically could expose overestimation of the EWS-eligible population cited when EWS reservations were introduced; Opposition leaders including Mallikarjun Kharge and Rahul Gandhi have questioned relying on an open-column methodology instead.

**Important points**
- Srinivas Goud and Backwards Classes Intellectual Forum chairman T.
- Chiranjeevulu argued the government should rationalise caste names from existing lists rather than drop them, and that refusing to enumerate OBC populations scientifically could expose overestimation of the EWS-eligible population cited when EWS reservations were introduced.
- Opposition leaders including Mallikarjun Kharge and Rahul Gandhi have questioned relying on an open-column methodology instead.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.10_

#### Emergency broadcasting of weather and natural disaster alerts resumed: C-DOT CEO

**In short:** C-DOT CEO Rajkumar Upadhyay told The Hindu that the emergency cell broadcasting service for extreme weather and natural disasters has "already resumed" after a brief suspension following an erroneous late-night alert in central Delhi. Developed by C-DOT and operated by State Disaster Management Agencies, the system required 300–350 adapters to reach diverse telecom equipment; Utimaco had sued the government over handing the project to C-DOT, and the Delhi High Court ruled in the government's favour while allowing the firm to pursue damages.

#### Violence breaks out in Arunachal over inclusions in SIR

**In short:** Alleged inclusion of Chakma and Hajong community members in the special intensive revision of electoral rolls sparked violence in Bordumsa, Changlang district, Arunachal Pradesh, on Friday, with firing and stone-pelting leaving at least one person injured, reportedly an All Arunachal Pradesh Students' Union member. The Buddhist Chakmas and Hindu Hajongs, displaced from erstwhile East Pakistan's Chittagong Hill Tracts and settled by the Centre in the 1960s, are opposed by AAPSU and local groups over inclusion of non-indigenous communities, especially those allegedly settled after 2006; AAPSU president Meje Taku demanded suspension of SIR in Chakma and Hajong areas, arrest of those who targeted protest leaders, and that no FIR be registered against AAPSU members.

#### Belgian port can be key to Indian exports: De Wever

**In short:** Belgian Prime Minister Bart De Wever, in Mumbai on Friday for a high-level dialogue on the India-EU Free Trade Agreement, pitched the Port of Antwerp-Bruges as a gateway for Indian exports to Europe in sectors including diamonds, pharmaceuticals, food processing, maritime services, green hydrogen and infrastructure. Union Commerce Minister Piyush Goyal invited Belgian companies to invest in India and said the FTA, under discussion for around 25 years and covering about two billion people and roughly $24 trillion in market value, would eliminate or reduce tariffs on more than 95% of Indian and European goods exports; bilateral trade stood at approximately $13.08 billion in 2025-26, with India-Belgium gems and jewellery trade at around $3.5 billion.

#### On Nepal Minister seeking damages, India says climate change a common challenge

**In short:** External Affairs Ministry spokesperson Randhir Jaiswal said on Friday that climate change is a "common challenge" and developed nations that are "historically" the highest emitters must lead in reducing emissions and supporting developing countries through finance and technology transfer. The remarks responded to Nepal Foreign Minister Shisir Khanal, who had said the U.S., China and India—as the three biggest greenhouse gas emitters—should bear responsibility for climate change and sought compensation after the August 26 flash flood; Khanal later denied seeking compensation and expressed "deep gratitude" for Indian help, while Jaiswal noted India has sent relief including tunnel rescue specialists and that Nepal Prime Minister Balendra Shah thanked India in Parliament.

#### Congress stands with 'all daughters' fighting for justice, says leader Alka Lamba

**In short:** All India Mahila Congress president Alka Lamba said on Friday that the Congress stands with "all the daughters" fighting for justice and will extend every possible support to them, citing the alleged assault of student activist Nishu Azad's father at a Jantar Mantar protest and cases involving journalists Shaheen Khan and Nafisa Khan and student activist Akriti Chaudhary, who was arrested under the National Security Act in Noida before the Allahabad High Court revoked it. She demanded action including provisions relating to attempted murder against self-styled "Hindutva influencer" Swatantra Bhardwaj, accused in the Azad matter, and called for suspension of Delhi Police personnel and release of CCTV footage after the journalists alleged they were detained and beaten while covering an event attended by Chief Minister Rekha Gupta and Union Home Minister Amit Shah.

The Congress is set to revamp its organisational structure in Uttar Pradesh ahead of next year's Assembly election, finalising a new set of District Congress Committee presidents as part of its ongoing organisational exercise. Venugopal said on Friday that detailed discussions over two days with the party's U.P. in-charge Rajendra Pal Gautam, Pradesh Congress Committee president Ajay Rai, CLP leader Aradhana Mishra and others focused on the Sangathan Srijan Abhiyan and election preparations, asserting that the 2024 Lok Sabha results showed voters wanted to "throw out the BJP" and that new appointments at all levels will begin this month.

#### 275 Indians still missing, 169 rescued, says Ministry

**In short:** The Kailash Manasarovar Yatra of 2026 has ended officially as all Indian pilgrims have left Chinese territory bordering Nepal following the August 26 flash flood in the Bhote Koshi river, the External Affairs Ministry said on Friday. Spokesperson Randhir Jaiswal confirmed that 275 Indian nationals remain missing and 169 Indians have been rescued, with numbers possibly fluctuating as survivors are still being discovered; apart from government-assisted tours, private travellers used the Nepali route through the Gyirong border crossing, where a significant number went missing when the port was hit by the flash flood.

#### Army kills one terrorist in forest area of J&K's Budgam

**In short:** One terrorist was killed on Friday during a joint operation by the Army and Jammu and Kashmir Police deep inside a forest area in Budgam district, officials said. The operation was launched based on information about the movement of a suspected group of terrorists from Pulwama district towards Budgam; based on specific intelligence input, the Indian Army, J&K Police and CRPF launched a joint search in Ashdar Gali, Budgam, and the operation is under way as forces are still looking for another terrorist believed to be holed up in the same area.

#### 82 educators set to receive National Teachers' Awards

**In short:** An asteroid hunter, a Maths wizard, a trailblazer in a coal mining community and a Special Olympics hockey coach are among 82 educators from across the country who will receive the National Teachers' Awards on Saturday, to be conferred by President Droupadi Murmu. Among the awardees are 48 school teachers, 21 faculty members in higher education, and 13 trainers from the Union Ministry of Skill Development and Entrepreneurship; notable recipients include Haryana-based Anil Kaushik, the 'Math Wizard'; Rajasthan-based Divyendu Sen, who trained students to track asteroids; Jaipur teacher Vartika Gulati; and Odisha-based Padaraj Nayak, whose students built a low-cost automatic plate-washing machine that earned a Government of India patent.

#### SC pushes for raising retirement age in district judiciary as backlog tops 5 cr.

**In short:** As 5.18 crore cases lie pending in district courts across the country, the Supreme Court has sent a distress call to States to enhance the retirement age of experienced judicial officers from 60 to 62 years, calling it the "crying need of the hour" to stop attrition of experienced judicial talent lest access to justice remain a mere chimera due to unfilled posts. Only seven States — Chhattisgarh, Karnataka, Madhya Pradesh, Maharashtra, Sikkim, Tamil Nadu and West Bengal — have responded positively; a three-judge Bench headed by Chief Justice of India Surya Kant directed them to amend service rules within two months and gave hesitant States, including Andhra Pradesh, Assam, Bihar, Gujarat, Kerala, Punjab, Rajasthan, Telangana, Uttar Pradesh and others, two weeks to decide on enhancement to 62 years.

#### Over 17,000 shifted to safer places amid floods in U.P.

**In short:** More than 17,500 people have been evacuated from 26 flood-affected districts across Uttar Pradesh, with no loss of life or livestock reported so far, the State government said on Friday. Eight rivers are flowing above the danger mark; around 3.53 lakh people have been affected, while 4,847 people are currently staying in relief shelters.

More than 17,500 people have been evacuated from 26 flood-affected districts across Uttar Pradesh, with no loss of life or livestock reported so far, the State government said on Friday. Eight rivers are flowing above the danger mark; around 3.53 lakh people have been affected, while 4,847 people are currently staying in relief shelters. The Relief Commissioner's office said 72,897 flood relief kits and more than 3.67 lakh lunch packets have been distributed, along with more than 5.52 lakh chlorine tablets and over 1.87 lakh ORS packets for health safety.

- **Concept:** Over 17,000 shifted to safer places amid floods in U.P

#### NTA seeks AICTE help to establish centres for computer-based tests

**In short:** The National Testing Agency has sought the help of the All India Council for Technical Education to map IT infrastructure and vacant spaces in government institutions to establish standard testing centres for computer-based testing for NTA examinations, including NEET. The NTA plans to shift NEET to CBT mode next year; nearly 22 lakh students appeared for NEET-UG 2026 in pen-and-paper format, which became embroiled in a paper leak controversy and had to be re-conducted.

The National Testing Agency has sought the help of the All India Council for Technical Education to map IT infrastructure and vacant spaces in government institutions to establish standard testing centres for computer-based testing for NTA examinations, including NEET. The NTA plans to shift NEET to CBT mode next year; nearly 22 lakh students appeared for NEET-UG 2026 in pen-and-paper format, which became embroiled in a paper leak controversy and had to be re-conducted. Following an NTA directive on August 5, AICTE wrote to government-run technical universities and heads of approved government institutes on August 31 to assess infrastructure, directing colleges and institutes to submit comprehensive facility data by September 15, including Internet connectivity, electrical backup and CCTV coverage.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** NTA seeks AICTE help to establish centres for computer-based tests
- **Told:** The National Testing Agency has sought the help of the All India Council for Technical Education to map IT infrastructure and vacant spaces in government institutions to establish standard testing centres for computer-based testing for NTA examinations, including NEET.
- **Purpose:** Following an NTA directive on August 5, AICTE wrote to government-run technical universities and heads of approved government institutes on August 31 to assess infrastructure, directing colleges and institutes to submit comprehensive facility data by September 15, including Internet connectivity, electrical backup and CCTV coverage.

**Important points**

#### Thermal sector grapples with coal stock management

**In short:** India's thermal power sector faces a fuel management dilemma in which generators maintaining adequate coal inventories can be disadvantaged when limited domestic supplies are redirected to plants that fall below prescribed stock norms. Industry stakeholders warn that repeated emergency redistribution, while sometimes needed for grid reliability, could undermine incentives to plan inventories under the CEA framework in effect since December 6, 2021 and the revised SHAKTI policy approved in May 2025.

India's thermal power sector faces a fuel management dilemma in which generators maintaining adequate coal inventories can be disadvantaged when limited domestic supplies are redirected to plants that fall below prescribed stock norms. Industry stakeholders warn that repeated emergency redistribution, while sometimes needed for grid reliability, could undermine incentives to plan inventories under the CEA framework in effect since December 6, 2021 and the revised SHAKTI policy approved in May 2025. National coal production exceeded one billion tonnes in each of the last two financial years and combined stocks at plants, pitheads and in transit stood around 148 MT, indicating the challenge is allocation and logistics rather than an overall shortage.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Thermal sector grapples with coal stock management
- **Told:** India's thermal power sector faces a fuel management dilemma in which generators maintaining adequate coal inventories can be disadvantaged when limited domestic supplies are redirected to plants that fall below prescribed stock norms.
- **Purpose:** National coal production exceeded one billion tonnes in each of the last two financial years and combined stocks at plants, pitheads and in transit stood around 148 MT, indicating the challenge is allocation and logistics rather than an overall shortage.

**Important points**
- National coal production exceeded one billion tonnes in each of the last two financial years and combined stocks at plants, pitheads and in transit stood around 148 MT, indicating the challenge is allocation and logistics rather than an overall shortage.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.13_

#### SEBI presses ahead against Hindenburg in Adani case

**In short:** India's markets regulator has started hearing representations as it seeks to recover gains from trades it suspects benefited from prior knowledge of Hindenburg Research's report on Adani Group, more than two years after the episode, according to two people with knowledge of the matter. SEBI believes it has jurisdiction although the parties are overseas because the trades were in India, and is proceeding on the grounds that they violated rules against trading on non-public information.

India's markets regulator has started hearing representations as it seeks to recover gains from trades it suspects benefited from prior knowledge of Hindenburg Research's report on Adani Group, more than two years after the episode, according to two people with knowledge of the matter. SEBI believes it has jurisdiction although the parties are overseas because the trades were in India, and is proceeding on the grounds that they violated rules against trading on non-public information. To secure assets for recovery, SEBI has opposed court-supervised insolvency proceedings in Mauritius for the Kotak K India Opportunities Fund Class F used to execute the trades.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** SEBI presses ahead against Hindenburg in Adani case
- **Told:** India's markets regulator has started hearing representations as it seeks to recover gains from trades it suspects benefited from prior knowledge of Hindenburg Research's report on Adani Group, more than two years after the episode, according to two people with knowledge of the matter.
- **Purpose:** To secure assets for recovery, SEBI has opposed court-supervised insolvency proceedings in Mauritius for the Kotak K India Opportunities Fund Class F used to execute the trades.

**Important points**
- To secure assets for recovery, SEBI has opposed court-supervised insolvency proceedings in Mauritius for the Kotak K India Opportunities Fund Class F used to execute the trades.

#### Douglas bullish about India at Asiad

**In short:** Troy Hamilton Douglas, head coach of athletics at the Inspire Institute of Sport (IIS), Vidhyanagar, Ballari, believes Indian athletics can better its performance at the 2026 Asian Games in Aichi-Nagoya after a record 29-medal haul at the Hangzhou Games in 2023 — six gold, 14 silver and nine bronze, India's best-ever athletics tally at the Asian Games since 1951. Douglas, whose contract with IIS runs until 2028, said he would be very happy if India equalled or won two or three more medals and projected the country could win 32 medals after observing Indian athletics for more than a year and watching the Indian Series this season.

Troy Hamilton Douglas, head coach of athletics at the Inspire Institute of Sport (IIS), Vidhyanagar, Ballari, believes Indian athletics can better its performance at the 2026 Asian Games in Aichi-Nagoya after a record 29-medal haul at the Hangzhou Games in 2023 — six gold, 14 silver and nine bronze, India's best-ever athletics tally at the Asian Games since 1951. Douglas, whose contract with IIS runs until 2028, said he would be very happy if India equalled or won two or three more medals and projected the country could win 32 medals after observing Indian athletics for more than a year and watching the Indian Series this season. Among IIS's 23 athletes, he singled out Rashdeep Kaur and Prachi Choudhary in the women's 400m and 4x400m relay as having a bright medal chance after proper preparation and a fantastic training camp with the national team.

**What this is**

#### Records galore on last day of Natl. youth athletics

**In short:** Several meet and national records tumbled on the final day of the 21st National youth athletics championships in Ludhiana on Thursday. Madhya Pradesh's Krishan Chandra won the boys' javelin with 79.92m, bettering the previous meet record of 77.41m set by Rohit Yadav in 2018 in Vadodara, while West Bengal's Misti Karmakar won the girls' javelin gold with 52.84m, surpassing the 51.84m set by Deepika in 2022.

Several meet and national records tumbled on the final day of the 21st National youth athletics championships in Ludhiana on Thursday. Madhya Pradesh's Krishan Chandra won the boys' javelin with 79.92m, bettering the previous meet record of 77.41m set by Rohit Yadav in 2018 in Vadodara, while West Bengal's Misti Karmakar won the girls' javelin gold with 52.84m, surpassing the 51.84m set by Deepika in 2022. Yash Mulik and Nupura Holla were declared the best male and female athletes respectively; Yash set a national record in the 110m hurdles with 13.40s. Haryana topped the medal tally with four gold, seven silver and three bronze; Maharashtra finished second with eight medals including three gold, and Karnataka finished third with three gold and two silver.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Records galore on last day of Natl. youth athletics
- **Told:** Several meet and national records tumbled on the final day of the 21st National youth athletics championships in Ludhiana on Thursday.
- **Purpose:** Haryana topped the medal tally with four gold, seven silver and three bronze; Maharashtra finished second with eight medals including three gold, and Karnataka finished third with three gold and two silver.

**Important points**
- Yash Mulik and Nupura Holla were declared the best male and female athletes respectively.
- Yash set a national record in the 110m hurdles with 13.40s.
- Haryana topped the medal tally with four gold, seven silver and three bronze.
- Maharashtra finished second with eight medals including three gold, and Karnataka finished third with three gold and two silver.

### International

#### Urine causes melting of Everest Base Camp: Study

**In short:** A new study estimates about 3 million kilograms of glacial ice and snow are lost each spring at Everest Base Camp from energy used for climber comforts, with human urine alone responsible for an estimated 154 tons of melt on the Khumbu Glacier. In 2023, researchers recorded 824 cylinders of liquefied petroleum gas, 18,935 litres of kerosene and 5,130 litres of gasoline used for cooking, heating and powering about 1,600 tents, while more than 6,000 litres of urine are produced daily by climbers and guides, most of it deposited directly on the glacier rather than in pit-vault toilets.

#### Pakistan rejects allegations of Hindu temple demolition

**In short:** Pakistan on Friday rejected allegations that a century-old Hindu temple in Siraj village, Narowal, Punjab, was deliberately demolished, asserting torrential rains on July 21 damaged its structure. Local people, minority bodies and a political party had said religious zealots demolished the temple to include its land in an adjacent seminary; India on Wednesday condemned the reported destruction and demanded Islamabad investigate and restore the temple, while Pakistan's Foreign Office called India's allegations "false and politically motivated."

#### Meloni celebrates govt record

**In short:** Giorgia Meloni on Friday celebrated her hard-right government becoming Italy's longest-lasting in the postwar republic, with a rally in Bari expected to draw thousands while protesters demonstrated nearby. Meloni, who became Italy's first woman PM on October 22, 2022, wrote on X that stability "is not a record to be displayed, but the condition that allows a nation to plan, decide, keep its commitments, and be respected."

#### India-born man on FBI list

**In short:** The FBI added India-born Manjit Singh Bedi, a naturalised US citizen, to its most-wanted fraudsters list for allegedly defrauding the US government of at least USD 6 lakh between March 2024 and June 2025 through his Tacoma, Washington grocery store. The FBI said Bedi swiped customers' SNAP Electronic Benefit Transfer cards to give them cash instead of food, keeping half the proceeds for himself.

#### Two workers rescued from tunnel in Nepal 9 days after flash floods

**In short:** Two men—Kabir Maharjan, 45, a mechanical supervisor, and Sanjay Sah, 30, a foreman—were rescued alive on Friday from the Trishuli 3A hydropower project tunnel nine days after flash floods tore through central Nepal's Trishuli River corridor. Maharjan was flown to Nepal Army Hospital in critical condition while Sah, who said he chanted the Hare Krishna mantra for nine days while trapped, is under treatment in Nuwakot; over 1,250 people have died and more than 4,700 remain missing from floods triggered by an August 26 ice-rock avalanche near the Nepal-Tibet border, with over 600 workers at 11 hydropower projects still unaccounted for.

#### Israel emptying refugee camps crime against humanity: UN

**In short:** The United Nations warned on Friday that Israel's forced displacement of more than 33,000 Palestinian refugees from Jenin, Nur Shams and Tulkarm camps during Operation "Iron Wall" launched in January 2025 in the occupied West Bank could amount to crimes against humanity of forcible transfer. The UN rights office report, slammed by Israel, described how Israeli forces killed civilians, destroyed hundreds of homes, cut water and electricity, barred humanitarian access and went door to door forcing residents to leave before consolidating control and displacing camp populations entirely.

#### UK asserts 'unshakeable' Falklands commitment

**In short:** Britain insisted Friday that the Falkland Islands "are British" after Argentine President Javier Milei said "winds of change" favoured Argentina's claim, citing Washington's readiness to review neutrality in the dispute and announcing sanctions on firms linked to oil extraction "in Argentine territory under British occupation." British Defence Secretary Wes Streeting responded that "The Falklands are British because Falkland Islanders choose to be British," after a British-Israeli oil project near the islands and Donald Trump's threat to wade into the dispute revived tensions dating to the 1982 war.

#### Pig kidney keeps patient alive until human transplant

**In short:** Tim Andrews, 66, received a genetically modified pig kidney at Mass General Brigham on January 25, 2025, and lived with it for nine months before becoming the first known patient to receive a human donor kidney after a pig kidney transplant. With roughly 90,000 people on the US kidney transplant waiting list and his rare blood type limiting options, Andrews' case provides early proof that pig kidneys from genetically edited animals could serve as "bridges" to human organ transplants while permanent xenotransplant solutions are developed.

#### Russia targets security chief's office in daylight Kyiv strike

**In short:** A Russian drone struck the SBU security service headquarters on Volodymyrska Street in Kyiv on Friday, with President Volodymyr Zelenskyy saying it was aimed directly at the head of the security service's personal office; acting SBU head Oleksandr Poklad was not in the vicinity. The daytime strike came two days before US envoy Steve Witkoff and Jared Kushner were expected to visit Kyiv to revive peace talks, while Witkoff and Kushner are also travelling to Moscow this weekend; Ukraine's Foreign Minister Andriy Sybiga called it a "major escalation."

#### Turning Point: Generation 9/11 — A timely 9/11 reckoning

**In short:** Brian Knappenberger's Netflix documentary 'Turning Point: Generation 9/11' shifts focus from 9/11 witnesses to those who grew up under its shadow, profiling children who lost parents and young Marines confronting the War on Terror. Through personal stories including Laurel Homer, whose father co-piloted Flight 93, it asks whether the War on Terror was worth it while tracing suspicion toward Muslims, divisive immigration politics and geopolitical ills rooted in the September 11 attacks.

#### Alaska glacier video falsely linked to Nepal floods

**In short:** A video showing a glacial outburst has gone viral on social media with claims that it captured a GLOF in China that triggered the devastating August 26 floods in Nepal. According to Alt News, a reverse-image search traced the footage to an Instagram reel posted on August 22 by US-based helicopter pilot Leigh Coates identifying the location as the Columbia Glacier in Alaska; the footage was recorded and posted days before the Nepal disaster and has no connection to the August 26 floods, and Alt News concluded the claim is false and misleading.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Alaska glacier video falsely linked to Nepal floods
- **Told:** A video showing a glacial outburst has gone viral on social media with claims that it captured a GLOF in China that triggered the devastating August 26 floods in Nepal.
- **Purpose:** According to Alt News, a reverse-image search traced the footage to an Instagram reel posted on August 22 by US-based helicopter pilot Leigh Coates identifying the location as the Columbia Glacier in Alaska; the footage was recorded and posted days before the Nepal disaster and has no connection to the August 26 floods, and Alt News concluded the claim is false and misleading.

#### Spain: 5 dead, dozens missing in a migrant boat

**In short:** At least five people have died and dozens more are feared dead after a boat carrying West African migrants was found drifting in the Atlantic, with forty-four people brought to safety in Spain's Canary Islands. Spain's Maritime Rescue Service said Thursday that a plane spotted a "precarious" boat about 120 km south of Gran Canaria on Wednesday afternoon; three survivors were evacuated by helicopter to hospital and the remaining forty-one migrants disembarked early Thursday in Arguineguin under Red Cross care.

At least five people have died and dozens more are feared dead after a boat carrying West African migrants was found drifting in the Atlantic, with forty-four people brought to safety in Spain's Canary Islands. Spain's Maritime Rescue Service said Thursday that a plane spotted a "precarious" boat about 120 km south of Gran Canaria on Wednesday afternoon; three survivors were evacuated by helicopter to hospital and the remaining forty-one migrants disembarked early Thursday in Arguineguin under Red Cross care. Survivors were disoriented and dehydrated men, some underage, from Mali or Senegal who reported leaving Gambia and being stranded at sea for many days, some estimating nearly a month, with more than 100 originally on board including women and a baby; remains of the five dead inside the boat could not be retrieved due to winds and 2.5-metre waves.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Spain: 5 dead, dozens missing in a migrant boat
- **Told:** At least five people have died and dozens more are feared dead after a boat carrying West African migrants was found drifting in the Atlantic, with forty-four people brought to safety in Spain's Canary Islands.
- **Purpose:** Survivors were disoriented and dehydrated men, some underage, from Mali or Senegal who reported leaving Gambia and being stranded at sea for many days, some estimating nearly a month, with more than 100 originally on board including women and a baby; remains of the five dead inside the boat could not be retrieved due to winds and 2.5-metre waves.

**Important points**
- Survivors were disoriented and dehydrated men, some underage, from Mali or Senegal who reported leaving Gambia and being stranded at sea for many days, some estimating nearly a month, with more than 100 originally on board including women and a baby.
- remains of the five dead inside the boat could not be retrieved due to winds and 2.5-metre waves.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.8_

#### Vance says Iran fight isn't war as Trump draws flak before polls

**In short:** Vice President JD Vance on Thursday rejected calling US fighting with Iran a "war," saying "right now, there is no active shooting," even as Iran fired at US Gulf ally Kuwait while retaliating for US strikes earlier in the week. Vance steered clear of predicting the six-month-old conflict would end before the November 3 midterm elections, saying the US had a "responsibility" to strike because Iran continues targeting commercial vessels in the Strait of Hormuz and that ending the conflict depends on when Iranians stop shooting at ships.

Vice President JD Vance on Thursday rejected calling US fighting with Iran a "war," saying "right now, there is no active shooting," even as Iran fired at US Gulf ally Kuwait while retaliating for US strikes earlier in the week. Vance steered clear of predicting the six-month-old conflict would end before the November 3 midterm elections, saying the US had a "responsibility" to strike because Iran continues targeting commercial vessels in the Strait of Hormuz and that ending the conflict depends on when Iranians stop shooting at ships. The White House had initially said the conflict would last weeks, but it has driven up gas prices and inflation as Republicans try to hold narrow congressional majorities; the administration in July also faced scrutiny after reclassifying four fallen soldiers and dozens of wounded troops in its Defence Casualty Analysis System.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Vance says Iran fight isn't war as Trump draws flak before polls
- **Told:** Vice President JD Vance on Thursday rejected calling US fighting with Iran a "war," saying "right now, there is no active shooting," even as Iran fired at US Gulf ally Kuwait while retaliating for US strikes earlier in the week.

- The White House had initially said the conflict would last weeks, but it has driven up gas prices and inflation as Republicans try to hold narrow congressional majorities.

#### Israel frees 4 more Lebanese prisoners in a deal

**In short:** Four prisoners from Lebanon held in Israel were released and handed to the International Committee of the Red Cross on Friday as part of a deal under which Lebanon agreed to search for bodies of Jewish Lebanese citizens missing since the civil war decades ago. A total of five civilian detainees are being released under the current deal; the first, Malek Ghazi, was freed to the ICRC and Lebanese army on Thursday, and AP journalists saw an ICRC convoy heading toward the Naqoura border crossing south of Tyre on Friday.

Four prisoners from Lebanon held in Israel were released and handed to the International Committee of the Red Cross on Friday as part of a deal under which Lebanon agreed to search for bodies of Jewish Lebanese citizens missing since the civil war decades ago. A total of five civilian detainees are being released under the current deal; the first, Malek Ghazi, was freed to the ICRC and Lebanese army on Thursday, and AP journalists saw an ICRC convoy heading toward the Naqoura border crossing south of Tyre on Friday. Mahmoud Ayash, brother of released prisoner Hussein Ayash, said his brother had been detained for more than two months and called from Naqoura to say he was with the ICRC; dozens of Lebanese prisoners including civilians and Hezbollah militants are believed held in Israeli detention centres.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Israel frees 4 more Lebanese prisoners in a deal
- **Told:** Four prisoners from Lebanon held in Israel were released and handed to the International Committee of the Red Cross on Friday as part of a deal under which Lebanon agreed to search for bodies of Jewish Lebanese citizens missing since the civil war decades ago.
- **Purpose:** Mahmoud Ayash, brother of released prisoner Hussein Ayash, said his brother had been detained for more than two months and called from Naqoura to say he was with the ICRC; dozens of Lebanese prisoners including civilians and Hezbollah militants are believed held in Israeli detention centres.

**Important points**
- Mahmoud Ayash, brother of released prisoner Hussein Ayash, said his brother had been detained for more than two months and called from Naqoura to say he was with the ICRC.
- dozens of Lebanese prisoners including civilians and Hezbollah militants are believed held in Israeli detention centres.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.8_

#### Russian attacks kill at least 3 in Ukraine

**In short:** Russian attacks overnight killed at least three people in Ukraine, local officials said Friday, as President Volodymyr Zelenskyy said US envoys are expected to visit Kyiv within the coming days amid renewed diplomatic efforts to end the war entering its fifth year. The air war has intensified with Russian forces striking civilian areas increasingly during the day as well as at night, while the front line in eastern and southern Ukraine remains largely deadlocked.

Russian attacks overnight killed at least three people in Ukraine, local officials said Friday, as President Volodymyr Zelenskyy said US envoys are expected to visit Kyiv within the coming days amid renewed diplomatic efforts to end the war entering its fifth year. The air war has intensified with Russian forces striking civilian areas increasingly during the day as well as at night, while the front line in eastern and southern Ukraine remains largely deadlocked. Zelenskyy said in his nightly address Thursday that preliminary dates have been set for meetings in both Moscow and Kyiv, with Ukraine's negotiating team in near-constant contact with the US side and expecting the Kyiv meeting in the coming days.

#### Many die in Nigeria oil theft attempt, inhale fumes

**In short:** At least 37 people died in Nigeria's oil-producing Rivers state while trying to steal oil from a loading vessel, the Youths and Environmental Advocacy Centre said Friday. The victims were simultaneously siphoning crude into their boats from an "illegal tapping point" on Thursday, inhaling toxic fumes at high pressure, with many still missing according to a volunteer network quoted in the report.

At least 37 people died in Nigeria's oil-producing Rivers state while trying to steal oil from a loading vessel, the Youths and Environmental Advocacy Centre said Friday. The victims were simultaneously siphoning crude into their boats from an "illegal tapping point" on Thursday, inhaling toxic fumes at high pressure, with many still missing according to a volunteer network quoted in the report. Nigerian police confirmed the incident on Friday but gave no death toll and said an investigation was underway; Rivers State police spokesperson Blessing Agabe said the victims were allegedly attempting to steal crude oil.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Many die in Nigeria oil theft attempt, inhale fumes
- **Told:** At least 37 people died in Nigeria's oil-producing Rivers state while trying to steal oil from a loading vessel, the Youths and Environmental Advocacy Centre said Friday.
- **Purpose:** Nigerian police confirmed the incident on Friday but gave no death toll and said an investigation was underway; Rivers State police spokesperson Blessing Agabe said the victims were allegedly attempting to steal crude oil.

**Important points**
- Nigerian police confirmed the incident on Friday but gave no death toll and said an investigation was underway.
- Rivers State police spokesperson Blessing Agabe said the victims were allegedly attempting to steal crude oil.

#### Security forces kill 48 militants in Balochistan

**In short:** Pakistan's army said Friday that at least 48 terrorists were neutralised across Balochistan in the last 48–72 hours during comprehensive anti-terrorist operations in Mastung, Quetta, Kalat and Sibi districts. In a Friday operation in Mastung based on intelligence inputs, security forces engaged terrorists and killed six; in Quetta's Shaban area, forces identified four Fitna al Khwarij hideouts and killed 26 terrorists in a fire exchange.

Pakistan's army said Friday that at least 48 terrorists were neutralised across Balochistan in the last 48–72 hours during comprehensive anti-terrorist operations in Mastung, Quetta, Kalat and Sibi districts. In a Friday operation in Mastung based on intelligence inputs, security forces engaged terrorists and killed six; in Quetta's Shaban area, forces identified four Fitna al Khwarij hideouts and killed 26 terrorists in a fire exchange. The Inter-Services Public Relations said caches of ammunition and improvised explosive devices recovered from militants were destroyed on-site.

#### Cyprus recovers 2 more bodies from ferry accident

**In short:** The remains of two more people have been recovered from the wreck of a passenger ferry that sank off Cyprus, bringing confirmed deaths to twelve with sixteen still missing, Prime Minister Unal Ustel of the island's northern territory said Friday. The Filo Jet was carrying 267 passengers and crew when it capsized and sank after leaving Kyrenia for Tasucu, Turkiye, on Sunday afternoon; two Turkish navy search and rescue vessels joined the search with submersibles able to reach wreckage about 550 metres below the surface.

The remains of two more people have been recovered from the wreck of a passenger ferry that sank off Cyprus, bringing confirmed deaths to twelve with sixteen still missing, Prime Minister Unal Ustel of the island's northern territory said Friday. The Filo Jet was carrying 267 passengers and crew when it capsized and sank after leaving Kyrenia for Tasucu, Turkiye, on Sunday afternoon; two Turkish navy search and rescue vessels joined the search with submersibles able to reach wreckage about 550 metres below the surface. Ustel said at Kyrenia that the newly recovered remains belonged to two women, after a man and a woman were recovered earlier.

#### Japanese motor-maker probe finds misconduct

**In short:** Scandal-ridden Japanese motor maker Nidec Corp. released Friday findings of a company investigation documenting 844 cases of misconduct related to quality controls over the last decade.

Scandal-ridden Japanese motor maker Nidec Corp. released Friday findings of a company investigation documenting 844 cases of misconduct related to quality controls over the last decade. Chief Executive Mitsuya Kishida bowed deeply at a news conference apologising to investors, group companies, customers and society, saying the entire company is working to review and improve quality controls. Most cases involved changing product materials or manufacturing processes without approval; others included faking product tests and false labelling, with company officials saying almost all were carried out by workers under intense pressure to achieve results.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Japanese motor-maker probe finds misconduct
- **Told:** Scandal-ridden Japanese motor maker Nidec Corp.
- **Purpose:** Most cases involved changing product materials or manufacturing processes without approval; others included faking product tests and false labelling, with company officials saying almost all were carried out by workers under intense pressure to achieve results.

**Important points**
- Chief Executive Mitsuya Kishida bowed deeply at a news conference apologising to investors, group companies, customers and society, saying the entire company is working to review and improve quality controls.
- Most cases involved changing product materials or manufacturing processes without approval.
- others included faking product tests and false labelling, with company officials saying almost all were carried out by workers under intense pressure to achieve results.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.8_

#### Denmark hopes to start deporting migrants to non-EU 'return hubs'

**In short:** Denmark said it hoped to begin sending rejected asylum seekers to non-European Union "return hubs" as early as 2027, ahead of a Friday Copenhagen meeting of ministers from Germany, Austria, The Netherlands and Greece leading European efforts to establish such centres outside the continent. In June, European lawmakers voted to allow member states to set up migrant detention centres outside the EU, enabling nations to negotiate deportation to facilities yet to be built in third countries rather than home countries; Danish Foreign Minister Lars Lokke Rasmussen said people without legal stay cannot continue residing in respective countries while irregular migrants should get a second chance in partner countries.

Denmark said it hoped to begin sending rejected asylum seekers to non-European Union "return hubs" as early as 2027, ahead of a Friday Copenhagen meeting of ministers from Germany, Austria, The Netherlands and Greece leading European efforts to establish such centres outside the continent. In June, European lawmakers voted to allow member states to set up migrant detention centres outside the EU, enabling nations to negotiate deportation to facilities yet to be built in third countries rather than home countries; Danish Foreign Minister Lars Lokke Rasmussen said people without legal stay cannot continue residing in respective countries while irregular migrants should get a second chance in partner countries. The five countries have been negotiating mainly with African governments to host rejected asylum seekers, with Denmark's Immigration and Integration Minister Morten Bods-kov saying talks have reached a crucial phase and Rwanda's government saying in early August it was natural to negotiate with European countries; migrant rights groups and Council of Europe Commissioner for Human Rights Michael O'Flaherty have criticised the plans.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Denmark hopes to start deporting migrants to non-EU 'return hubs'
- **Told:** Denmark said it hoped to begin sending rejected asylum seekers to non-European Union "return hubs" as early as 2027, ahead of a Friday Copenhagen meeting of ministers from Germany, Austria, The Netherlands and Greece leading European efforts to establish such centres outside the continent.
- **Purpose:** The five countries have been negotiating mainly with African governments to host rejected asylum seekers, with Denmark's Immigration and Integration Minister Morten Bods-kov saying talks have reached a crucial phase and Rwanda's government saying in early August it was natural to negotiate with European countries; migrant rights groups and Council of Europe Commissioner for Human Rights Michael O'Flaherty have criticised the plans.

**Important points**
- The five countries have been negotiating mainly with African governments to host rejected asylum seekers, with Denmark's Immigration and Integration Minister Morten Bods-kov saying talks have reached a crucial phase and Rwanda's government saying in early August it was natural to negotiate with European countries.
- migrant rights groups and Council of Europe Commissioner for Human Rights Michael O'Flaherty have criticised the plans.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.8_

#### Tehran strikes Jordan, tests its bonds with US

**In short:** Jordan, a staunch US ally hosting American forces, has paid a price for its Washington relationship as its military bases become repeat targets of Iranian strikes, with hundreds of drone and missile attacks adding to economic strains and volatility in the neighbouring West Bank where Israeli settlers have ramped up attacks on Palestinians. Despite the risk of more strikes, Jordanian leaders say they remain committed to maintaining the relationship after decades of deeper security ties with American officials, with analysts saying the alliance helps guard against Iranian attempts to destabilise neighbours.

Jordan, a staunch US ally hosting American forces, has paid a price for its Washington relationship as its military bases become repeat targets of Iranian strikes, with hundreds of drone and missile attacks adding to economic strains and volatility in the neighbouring West Bank where Israeli settlers have ramped up attacks on Palestinians. Despite the risk of more strikes, Jordanian leaders say they remain committed to maintaining the relationship after decades of deeper security ties with American officials, with analysts saying the alliance helps guard against Iranian attempts to destabilise neighbours. King Abdullah II of Jordan last year acknowledged the need for such alliances, warning that destabilisation was fuelled by the COVID-19 crisis.
