#### Modi hails Meloni after Italian leader breaks record

**In short:** Prime Minister Narendra Modi congratulated Giorgia Meloni on Friday as she became Italy's longest continuously serving post-war Prime Minister after 1,413 days in office since October 22, 2022, calling it a reflection of Italian people's trust in her leadership. Ms.

Prime Minister Narendra Modi congratulated Giorgia Meloni on Friday as she became Italy's longest continuously serving post-war Prime Minister after 1,413 days in office since October 22, 2022, calling it a reflection of Italian people's trust in her leadership. Ms. Meloni, Italy's first woman Prime Minister, thanked Mr. Modi and said India–Italy ties are stronger than ever as the two countries work to strengthen Europe–Asia links and develop the India-West Asia-Europe Economic Corridor; Mr. Modi said her friendship had helped strengthen the India-Italy Special Strategic Partnership.

#### Indian volunteers take relief to remote Nepal communities

**In short:** Nearly a week after devastating floods destroyed homes and cut roads in Nepal, Indian volunteers including Khalsa Aid teams are travelling up to 10 hours through damaged mountain roads and bridges to deliver food, bedding and household essentials to remote communities such as Trishuli and Tupche, while some areas of Rasuwa remain accessible only by helicopter at about $2,000 per trip. Volunteer Manohar Naidu said mass burials are being carried out as mortuaries run out of space, with ten Indian volunteers currently handling logistics, assessments, packing and distribution as demand outstrips local availability of mattresses, solar lights and other household items.

#### 'India working to draw out ideas to end Ukraine war'

**In short:** India has been in continuous touch with both the Ukrainian and Russian sides and is trying to see if there are ideas and suggestions which can be developed in some way that will contribute to the mitigation or cessation of the conflict, External Affairs Minister S. Jaishankar said on Friday.

India has been in continuous touch with both the Ukrainian and Russian sides and is trying to see if there are ideas and suggestions which can be developed in some way that will contribute to the mitigation or cessation of the conflict, External Affairs Minister S. Jaishankar said on Friday. Jaishankar, who arrived in Warsaw from Kyiv where he held talks with top Ukrainian leadership on Thursday, made the remarks after meeting Poland's Deputy Prime Minister and Foreign Minister Radosław Sikorski; he had said in Ukraine that India is ready to extend "any help in any manner" to end the Ukraine-Russia war. After talks with Jaishankar, Zelenskyy said he was "grateful to India for its commitment to ending this unjust war."

**Important points**

#### Tata Chemicals' Kenya unit ordered to close; firm says all rules followed

**In short:** Kenya's government has ordered the closure of Tata Chemicals Magadi Ltd., Tata Chemicals Ltd.'s soda ash subsidiary, for "non-contribution to the local economy," with President William Ruto reportedly directing that two new firms be roped in to take over operations. Tata Chemicals said it received official communication dated July 28, 2026, submitted all required documentation on August 11 and is fully compliant while awaiting the ministry's review.

#### More than 120 killed as Yemen's Houthis, government forces clash

**In short:** A major offensive by Yemen's Houthis to seize control of the gateway to the Red Sea has left more than 120 people dead, sources said on Friday, in the country's deadliest clashes in years. Fighting erupted on Thursday after the Iran-backed Houthis launched a ground assault with rocket and drone attacks near coastal areas, vying for control of the Bab el-Mandeb strait; the toll rose to 129 on Friday, including 66 government troops, at least 62 Houthi fighters and one civilian, as the rebels sought to cut off the government-held port city of Mokha and advance toward areas bordering Bab el-Mandeb.

#### UN rights chief calls for end to Sri Lanka 'impunity'

**In short:** The UN Human Rights chief has said Sri Lanka needs to do more to protect human rights, advance accountability, and end the "long legacy of impunity", in an Office of the High Commissioner for Human Rights report released on Thursday as President Anura Kumara Dissanayake completes two years in office. The report acknowledged the government's "notable steps" on corruption, high-profile arrests and investigations into political killings, enforced disappearances and the 2019 Easter Sunday attacks, but said meaningful progress remains "largely absent" on accountability for international crimes allegedly committed by State security forces and the LTTE, and urged a moratorium on the Prevention of Terrorism Act pending its repeal.

### Sports

#### Running at dawn, Mumbai cabby eyes 100 medals for India

**In short:** Mumbai cab driver Parag Patil, 47, trains before dawn and drives passengers more than 12 hours a day while pursuing a goal of 100-plus international masters athletics medals—he already holds 22, including four gold, across India, Australia, New Zealand, Italy and Malaysia. He is preparing for the Pan-Pacific Masters Games on Australia's Gold Coast from November 6–15 in the 45–49 age group, though expenses of Rs 6–7 lakh have forced him to take loans and crowdfund, and his wife Sunita twice mortgaged her mangalsutra before it had to be sold.

#### Zverev burns midnight oil

**In short:** Third seed Felix Auger-Aliassime exited the US Open on Thursday as Karen Khachanov won 6-7 (5/7), 6-3, 6-2, 6-2, capitalising on 47 unforced errors from the dizzy Canadian, while top seed Alexander Zverev needed 4hr 33min to beat Quentin Halys 6-4, 4-6, 7-6 (3), 6-7 (3), 6-3 at just after 2.15am. Former champions Iga Swiatek, Naomi Osaka and Coco Gauff reached the third round, sixth seed Alex de Minaur lost to Botic van de Zandschulp, and Madison Keys saved five match points to beat Anna Bondar.

#### India to play Japan in historic T20I contest

**In short:** India's men's team will play Japan in their first-ever T20 International on September 22 at Sano International Cricket Ground in Tochigi Prefecture, launching celebrations for the 75th anniversary of India-Japan diplomatic relations and offering game time before the Asian Games in Aichi-Nagoya from September 19 to October 4. The sides will compete for the Suzuki Cup, the BCCI and Japan Cricket Association announced.

#### SAI's 'container simulator' prepares Indian athletes for unusual Asian Games

**In short:** Sports Authority of India has set up makeshift shipping-container rooms at NSNIS Patiala and Bengaluru so athletes can acclimatise to the unconventional accommodation awaiting them at the Aichi-Nagoya Asian Games beginning September 19, which will have no conventional athletes' village. Athletes take turns spending a night inside the roughly six-foot beds with close walls and limited space, then follow their usual training routine the next morning so the arrangement is no longer a distraction in Japan.

Sports Authority of India has set up makeshift shipping-container rooms at NSNIS Patiala and Bengaluru so athletes can acclimatise to the unconventional accommodation awaiting them at the Aichi-Nagoya Asian Games beginning September 19, which will have no conventional athletes' village. Athletes take turns spending a night inside the roughly six-foot beds with close walls and limited space, then follow their usual training routine the next morning so the arrangement is no longer a distraction in Japan. Deputy Director General Vineet Kumar said the Sports Minister's vision was to simulate container and cruise-style lodging two years ago when the unique accommodation plan was established.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** SAI's 'container simulator' prepares Indian athletes for unusual Asian Games

- **Purpose:** Deputy Director General Vineet Kumar said the Sports Minister's vision was to simulate container and cruise-style lodging two years ago when the unique accommodation plan was established.

#### Kidambi Srikanth ousted in China Masters

**In short:** Kidambi Srikanth's China Masters Super 750 run ended in the quarterfinals on Friday as the former world No. 1 lost 16-21, 16-21 in 39 minutes to Hong Kong's 25th-ranked Lee Cheuk Yiu in Shenzhen.

Kidambi Srikanth's China Masters Super 750 run ended in the quarterfinals on Friday as the former world No. 1 lost 16-21, 16-21 in 39 minutes to Hong Kong's 25th-ranked Lee Cheuk Yiu in Shenzhen. The 33-year-old Indian, runner-up at the US Open Super 300 earlier this year, had beaten world No. 9 Victor Lai 21-18, 21-19 in the Round of 16 but chased the game for long stretches against Lee Cheuk, who rallied from 3-3 in the opener with a six-point burst to 14-6 and held his composure in a tighter second game that reached 15-15.

#### Zverev survives; Khachanov knocks out Auger-Aliassime

**In short:** Top-seeded Alexander Zverev kept his bid for a first US Open title on track with a gritty 6-4, 4-6, 7-6(3), 6-7(3), 6-3 victory over Quentin Halys in a four-hour 33-minute marathon that ended at 2.15 a.m. on Friday, while men's third seed Felix Auger-Aliassime fell 6-7(5), 6-3, 6-2, 6-2 to 50th-ranked Karen Khachanov after saying he was dizzy and seeing spots; sixth seed Alex de Minaur and 13th seed Lorenzo Musetti also lost, while Coco Gauff, Iga Swiatek, Naomi Osaka, Elena Rybakina and Mirra Andreeva advanced in the women's draw.

#### Mature Gurindervir battles stopwatch, questions, injuries

**In short:** India's fastest man Gurindervir Singh, 25, the only Indian to go under 10.1s with a national record of 10.09s set in May, has endured a torrid few months after a hamstring injury, a Commonwealth Games first-round exit, and a seventh-place finish at the World Athletics Continental Tour in Bhubaneswar. Now training at the Reliance Foundation high performance centre in Navi Mumbai under athletics director James Hillier ahead of the Asian Games, he is working to carry his strong first 60–70 metres through the full 100m, with a Delhi competition on September 10 set as a key test of his form.

India's fastest man Gurindervir Singh, 25, the only Indian to go under 10.1s with a national record of 10.09s set in May, has endured a torrid few months after a hamstring injury, a Commonwealth Games first-round exit, and a seventh-place finish at the World Athletics Continental Tour in Bhubaneswar. Now training at the Reliance Foundation high performance centre in Navi Mumbai under athletics director James Hillier ahead of the Asian Games, he is working to carry his strong first 60–70 metres through the full 100m, with a Delhi competition on September 10 set as a key test of his form. Gurindervir says he has evolved from the stressed athlete who broke the national record in 10.20s at the Indian Grand Prix 1 in March 2025 despite stomach ulcers and weight loss, and remains determined to prove Indians can be competitive in the 100m.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Mature Gurindervir battles stopwatch, questions, injuries
- **Told:** India's fastest man Gurindervir Singh, 25, the only Indian to go under 10.1s with a national record of 10.09s set in May, has endured a torrid few months after a hamstring injury, a Commonwealth Games first-round exit, and a seventh-place finish at the World Athletics Continental Tour in Bhubaneswar.
- **Purpose:** Gurindervir says he has evolved from the stressed athlete who broke the national record in 10.20s at the Indian Grand Prix 1 in March 2025 despite stomach ulcers and weight loss, and remains determined to prove Indians can be competitive in the 100m.

#### I have a choice to live with gratitude or anger; I choose gratitude

**In short:** Former American middle-distance runner Shannon Rowbury, who finished sixth in the women's 1500m at London 2012, will receive her Olympic bronze at LA28 after five competitors ahead of her — including gold and silver medallists Asli Cakir Alptekin and Gamze Bulut of Turkey and Russian Tatyana Tomashova — were disqualified for doping over the following 12–13 years. In an interview during an Athletics Federation of India initiative in Bhubaneswar, where she trained aspiring commentators and worked at the World Athletics Continental Tour Silver meet, Rowbury spoke about competing in an era tainted by doping, her emotional reaction on learning she would get justice, her work with USADA on clean-sport education, and her shift from a decade in the world top ten to broadcasting.

#### Tracking the elusive snow leopards

**In short:** When Kulbhushansingh Suryawanshi heard how his grandfather shot down a tiger that had entered his village, he could not reconcile himself to the fact and felt the tiger's life should have been spared. Today he is one among a handful of snow leopard conservationists, and he shares his story.

#### The teacher who forgot everything

**In short:** On Teachers' Day, Class 5B's Mrs Meera Rao pretends she has forgotten her students and her lessons, prompting the children to teach her back what she taught them—about looking up unknown words, asking questions without embarrassment, disagreeing without becoming enemies, and that marks do not define who you are. When Kavya offers her Teachers' Day card saying people sometimes need reminding of what they already know, Mrs Meera reveals she has not forgotten anything and wanted to know whether her lessons had stayed with them.

#### Should children keep a journal? (NO)

**In short:** Jayden Robert, 14, of Hubballi, Karnataka, argues universal journal encouragement can turn self-reflection into an anxiety-inducing chore and that children co-regulate emotions through talking to elders, friends and teachers rather than notebooks. He warns that parents tempted to read private diaries can damage trust, and that talking provides support and a safety net better than writing alone for children facing depression or suicidal thoughts.

#### Grow your salt crystals

**In short:** An Open Sesame experiment using a glass jar, warm water, salt, a paper clip, pencil and string shows how salt crystals form on a paper clip suspended in saturated salt water as the water evaporates. As evaporation proceeds, dissolved salt can no longer stay in solution and crystallises on the clip over several days.

#### Floating and sinking egg

**In short:** An Open Sesame experiment placing raw eggs in plain water versus water with 5–6 tablespoons of dissolved salt demonstrates that an egg sinks in plain water but floats in salty water because salt water is denser than the egg. In plain water, the egg is denser and sinks to the bottom.

#### A complete guide to healthy eating in the rainy season

**In short:** Monsoon humidity, moisture and sudden temperature drops create ideal conditions for airborne bacteria, water-borne pathogens and fungal spores, driving seasonal flus, respiratory infections and gastrointestinal illnesses such as food poisoning and gastroenteritis. High humidity weakens digestive capacity by disrupting circadian rhythms, reducing digestive enzyme secretion and slowing gastric motility, leaving the gut vulnerable to indigestion, bloating, acidity and metabolic sluggishness.

Monsoon humidity, moisture and sudden temperature drops create ideal conditions for airborne bacteria, water-borne pathogens and fungal spores, driving seasonal flus, respiratory infections and gastrointestinal illnesses such as food poisoning and gastroenteritis. High humidity weakens digestive capacity by disrupting circadian rhythms, reducing digestive enzyme secretion and slowing gastric motility, leaving the gut vulnerable to indigestion, bloating, acidity and metabolic sluggishness. Core dietary guidance includes light, easily digestible foods such as white or parboiled rice, oats, barley, puffed rice and sooji, split yellow moong dal instead of whole legumes, antimicrobial spices including turmeric paired with black pepper, ginger, garlic, cinnamon, clove and carom seeds, and freshly cooked piping hot meals reheated thoroughly if left at room temperature for more than an hour, while avoiding heavy fats and fried foods.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** A complete guide to healthy eating in the rainy season
- **Told:** Monsoon humidity, moisture and sudden temperature drops create ideal conditions for airborne bacteria, water-borne pathogens and fungal spores, driving seasonal flus, respiratory infections and gastrointestinal illnesses such as food poisoning and gastroenteritis.
- **Purpose:** Core dietary guidance includes light, easily digestible foods such as white or parboiled rice, oats, barley, puffed rice and sooji, split yellow moong dal instead of whole legumes, antimicrobial spices including turmeric paired with black pepper, ginger, garlic, cinnamon, clove and carom seeds, and freshly cooked piping hot meals reheated thoroughly if left at room temperature for more than an hour, while avoiding heavy fats and fried foods.

**Important points**
- Core dietary guidance includes light, easily digestible foods such as white or parboiled rice, oats, barley, puffed rice and sooji, split yellow moong dal instead of whole legumes, antimicrobial spices including turmeric paired with black pepper, ginger, garlic, cinnamon, clove and carom seeds, and freshly cooked piping hot meals reheated thoroughly if left at room temperature for more than an hour, while avoiding heavy fats and fried foods.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.7_

#### Ozempic for teens: What parents must know before considering treatment

**In short:** Semaglutide and related GLP-1 medications are weekly injections that mimic a gut hormone regulating appetite and insulin release, and while they can produce dramatic results in adults with obesity or type 2 diabetes, the calculation differs for adolescents whose bone density, metabolic set points and relationship with food are still developing. A physician writing about a sixteen-year-old whose mother asked about starting treatment after rapid post-thyroid weight gain warns that medication-driven weight loss in a growing body can take lean muscle along with fat, potentially leaving a teenager lighter but metabolically worse off without a plan to preserve muscle.

Semaglutide and related GLP-1 medications are weekly injections that mimic a gut hormone regulating appetite and insulin release, and while they can produce dramatic results in adults with obesity or type 2 diabetes, the calculation differs for adolescents whose bone density, metabolic set points and relationship with food are still developing. A physician writing about a sixteen-year-old whose mother asked about starting treatment after rapid post-thyroid weight gain warns that medication-driven weight loss in a growing body can take lean muscle along with fat, potentially leaving a teenager lighter but metabolically worse off without a plan to preserve muscle. Before considering GLP-1 drugs, parents should ensure the cause of weight gain has been properly worked up, a structured nutrition and resistance training plan has been genuinely tried, and a physician will monitor muscle mass and growth over time; in one case, bloodwork and insulin sensitivity testing changed the plan before any injection was discussed.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Ozempic for teens: What parents must know before considering treatment
- **Told:** Semaglutide and related GLP-1 medications are weekly injections that mimic a gut hormone regulating appetite and insulin release, and while they can produce dramatic results in adults with obesity or type 2 diabetes, the calculation differs for adolescents whose bone density, metabolic set points and relationship with food are still developing.
- **Purpose:** Before considering GLP-1 drugs, parents should ensure the cause of weight gain has been properly worked up, a structured nutrition and resistance training plan has been genuinely tried, and a physician will monitor muscle mass and growth over time; in one case, bloodwork and insulin sensitivity testing changed the plan before any injection was discussed.

**Important points**
- Before considering GLP-1 drugs, parents should ensure the cause of weight gain has been properly worked up, a structured nutrition and resistance training plan has been genuinely tried, and a physician will monitor muscle mass and growth over time.
- in one case, bloodwork and insulin sensitivity testing changed the plan before any injection was discussed.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.7_

#### The science-backed benefits of raw milk

**In short:** Unpasteurized raw milk is described as a dense nutrient reservoir of natural alpha-hydroxy acids, bio-available vitamins, essential proteins and healthy lipids that can topically soothe, hydrate and brighten skin without synthetic chemicals. Lactic acid provides gentle chemical exfoliation by breaking down desmosomes holding dead skin cells, while natural fats and proteins lock in moisture; regular application may fade tan lines and hyperpigmentation, soothe sunburn and inflammation via vitamins A and D plus zinc and magnesium, and cleanse pores by binding excess sebum without stripping the acid mantle.

Suggested uses include a daily hydrating cleanser, a brightening besan and wild turmeric pack, and a chilled compress for irritated skin, with a patch test advised especially for acne-prone skin or dairy allergies because unpasteurized milk carries naturally occurring bacteria.

**What this is**

#### Elliot Page says not getting lot of casting calls post 'The Odyssey'

**In short:** Actor Elliot Page, who played Ithacan soldier Sinon in Christopher Nolan's "The Odyssey", says he is not getting many casting calls after the film, which has become the second highest-grossing movie of the year. In an interview with On Film...

Actor Elliot Page, who played Ithacan soldier Sinon in Christopher Nolan's "The Odyssey", says he is not getting many casting calls after the film, which has become the second highest-grossing movie of the year. In an interview with On Film... With Kevin McCarthy, Page said he has no other job lined up and plans to be more proactive reaching out to directors, noting he "was seen in a certain way before and it is different now" after coming out as a trans man in 2021; Page, formerly Ellen Page and previously in Nolan's "Inception", faced right-wing social media backlash with many assuming he was cast as Achilles. In the film, Page appears in early scenes as Sinon, left with the Trojan horse while Odysseus (Matt Damon) does not reveal that he and others are hiding inside.

#### I'll enjoy watching it on big screen, says Shriya on 'Mirzapur: The Movie'

**In short:** Actor Shriya Pilgaonkar returns as Sweety in "Mirzapur: The Movie", the cinematic extension of the crime series that debuted in cinemas nationwide on Friday as the franchise's first big-screen outing after launching as a streaming series in 2018 and running three seasons. Pilgaonkar, reprising Swaragini "Sweety" Gupta from season one despite her character's death in that finale, called the film an "experiment" and said reuniting with the cast felt like a party; the movie is set in 2018 and hints at an untold chapter from season one, bringing back Ali Fazal as Guddu Pandit, Pankaj Tripathi as Kaleen Bhaiya, Divyenndu as Munna Tripathi, Rasika Dugal as Beena Tripathi, Shweta Tripathi as Golu Gupta and Abhishek Banerjee as Compounder.

She said fans' sustained affection for Sweety has carried the team through, recalling that season one reviews were not very good but the show gained a cult following over time.

#### Don't believe in boxing myself, says Abhay Verma

**In short:** "Munjya" and "Operation Safed Sagar" star Abhay Verma says he consciously avoids being boxed into one image, seeking roles that let him reinvent himself after starting with a transgender role in "Safed", playing a sleeper cell operative in "Family Man", and doing horror-comedy "Munjya". #### 'Hanuman Ansh' set for worldwide release on Sept 11

**In short:** Hombale Films announced Thursday it will take the Hindi film "Hanuman Ansh", inspired by spiritual leader Neem Karoli Baba, to international audiences with a worldwide release on September 11, 2026, through its distribution arm Hombale Overseas. Written and directed by Vishal Chaturvedi, the film follows Dr Ram Bhosle, an Indian freedom fighter whose journey from underground rebel to yogi unfolds under Maharaj-ji's guidance, exploring faith, service and inner transformation.

Hombale Films announced Thursday it will take the Hindi film "Hanuman Ansh", inspired by spiritual leader Neem Karoli Baba, to international audiences with a worldwide release on September 11, 2026, through its distribution arm Hombale Overseas. Written and directed by Vishal Chaturvedi, the film follows Dr Ram Bhosle, an Indian freedom fighter whose journey from underground rebel to yogi unfolds under Maharaj-ji's guidance, exploring faith, service and inner transformation. Released in Indian theatres on August 7 with a slow start before gaining word-of-mouth momentum, it has earned Rs 72.97 crore gross and Rs 61.88 crore nett according to trade website Sacnilk.

#### PRIYANKA Jonas to present Karl Lagerfeld Prize at LVMH Ceremony

**In short:** Actor Priyanka Chopra Jonas has been named an ambassador for the 2026 LVMH Prize final and will present the Karl Lagerfeld Prize at the ceremony at the Fondation Louis Vuitton in Paris, joining fellow Dior ambassador Jennifer Lawrence and Louis Vuitton face Urassaya "Yaya" Sperbund as presenters. Chopra Jonas will present the Karl Lagerfeld Prize carrying a grant of EUR 200,000 (one euro = Rs 109.87) and a year-long mentorship programme covering sustainability, communication, marketing, manufacturing and financial management; Lawrence will present the EUR 400,000 Grand Prize and Sperbund the Savoir-Faire Prize.

Chopra Jonas will present the Karl Lagerfeld Prize carrying a grant of EUR 200,000 (one euro = Rs 109.87) and a year-long mentorship programme covering sustainability, communication, marketing, manufacturing and financial management; Lawrence will present the EUR 400,000 Grand Prize and Sperbund the Savoir-Faire Prize. Nine designers — Julie Kegels, Petra Fagerstrom, De Pino, Ponte, The Vxlley, Institution, Lii, Colleen Allen and Yoshita 1967 — compete before a jury including Celine's Michael Rider, Loewe's Jack McColough and Lazaro Hernandez, Pucci's Camille Miceli, and Louis Vuitton and LVMH Fashion Group chairman Pietro Beccari.

#### 'Marriage should not mean that only women adjust'

**In short:** Tamil actors Elan and Saanve Megghana say their Netflix film "Pyaar Prema Kalyanam", which premiered on August 21, was designed to start conversation about where a couple will live after marriage by reversing the norm so the husband moves into his wife's family home. Directed by and starring Elan opposite Megghana as social media influencer Pavithra, the romantic comedy follows a woman who refuses to leave her parental home after her wedding; Elan said the aim was to explore the issue rather than preach, and Megghana said the premise is treated with humour rather than as a message-driven social drama.

Tamil actors Elan and Saanve Megghana say their Netflix film "Pyaar Prema Kalyanam", which premiered on August 21, was designed to start conversation about where a couple will live after marriage by reversing the norm so the husband moves into his wife's family home. Directed by and starring Elan opposite Megghana as social media influencer Pavithra, the romantic comedy follows a woman who refuses to leave her parental home after her wedding; Elan said the aim was to explore the issue rather than preach, and Megghana said the premise is treated with humour rather than as a message-driven social drama. Elan, who also directed the 2024 hit "Star", said the story by Mumbai-based writer Nivedita Pohankar was shared in casual conversation before he encouraged her to develop it into a script to show women's perspective on adjustment expectations in Indian households.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** 'Marriage should not mean that only women adjust'

#### Rooney and Kate Mara had to speak in unison

**In short:** Sisters Rooney and Kate Mara play fictional sisters Jean and Joan Holbrooke who speak in unison in Werner Herzog's "Bucking Fastard", which premiered at the Venice Film Festival and received a nearly seven-minute standing ovation but has no US release date yet. Herzog, 83, wrote the script in five days after someone suggested the Mara sisters instead of twins he had met decades earlier, and cut planned two-week rehearsal to five days because the sisters picked up unison speech so quickly; Orlando Bloom plays the object of both sisters' affection.

Sisters Rooney and Kate Mara play fictional sisters Jean and Joan Holbrooke who speak in unison in Werner Herzog's "Bucking Fastard", which premiered at the Venice Film Festival and received a nearly seven-minute standing ovation but has no US release date yet. Rooney Mara said playing the same person was daunting but their shared history made it natural, Kate Mara called it "very musical," and Herzog dedicated the film to the late David Lynch, saying he wished Lynch could have been the first audience to see it.

#### Gugga Navami

**In short:** The day after Janmashtami is observed as Gugga Naumi (Navami), a popular folk religious festival celebrated across Rajasthan, Haryana, Punjab, Jammu and Kashmir, Uttar Pradesh and other parts of north India. Gugga Naumi reflects the syncretic and fluid traditions of India, where He is worshipped by people of different religions and sects.

The day after Janmashtami is observed as Gugga Naumi (Navami), a popular folk religious festival celebrated across Rajasthan, Haryana, Punjab, Jammu and Kashmir, Uttar Pradesh and other parts of north India. Gugga Naumi reflects the syncretic and fluid traditions of India, where He is worshipped by people of different religions and sects. The celebration begins on the day of Rakhi and continues for nine days, starting with pilgrimage to Gugga Medi village in Hanumangarh district of Rajasthan; devotional songs called 'pir ke solle' are sung by gugga mandlis, and prayers and fairs take place at Gugga's shrines, called marhis, dotted across villages in north India.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** Gugga Navami
- **Told:** The day after Janmashtami is observed as Gugga Naumi (Navami), a popular folk religious festival celebrated across Rajasthan, Haryana, Punjab, Jammu and Kashmir, Uttar Pradesh and other parts of north India.

**Important points**
- The celebration begins on the day of Rakhi and continues for nine days, starting with pilgrimage to Gugga Medi village in Hanumangarh district of Rajasthan.
- devotional songs called 'pir ke solle' are sung by gugga mandlis, and prayers and fairs take place at Gugga's shrines, called marhis, dotted across villages in north India.

#### Electric heels

**In short:** The second edition of Australia's Electric Sports Racing Association Nationals was held in Coffs Harbour last month, featuring nascent electric racing disciplines including electric skateboarding, electric unicycling and one-wheeling on purpose-built circuits. Battery-run electric skateboards, typically controlled by a wireless throttle or the rider's weight and steered by tilting, can reportedly reach 30–40 kmph or higher with powerful motors; while environment-friendly, they have drawn safety concerns that the ESRA regulatory body is addressing through enforced safety guidelines.

### Other

#### 'Lau Suna Suna' and why context matters

**In short:** As floods devastated parts of Nepal and Tibet, a clip from the 2021 short film 'The Silent Echo' went viral on social media, featuring a young girl singing 'Lau Suna Suna' in Nepali about hardship in the mountains. Director Suman Sen said the trend strips the song of its context, with clips repurposed for travel reels, outfit checks and club playlists when it is about mountain hardships; Saurav Rai wrote the lyrics, Mayukh Mainak composed the music, and Dipali Pradhan sang the trending version.

As floods devastated parts of Nepal and Tibet, a clip from the 2021 short film 'The Silent Echo' went viral on social media, featuring a young girl singing 'Lau Suna Suna' in Nepali about hardship in the mountains. Director Suman Sen said the trend strips the song of its context, with clips repurposed for travel reels, outfit checks and club playlists when it is about mountain hardships; Saurav Rai wrote the lyrics, Mayukh Mainak composed the music, and Dipali Pradhan sang the trending version. Sen urged listeners to empathise with the broader message about indifference to nature rather than treat the song as a folk elegy miscredited online.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** 'Lau Suna Suna' and why context matters
- **Told:** As floods devastated parts of Nepal and Tibet, a clip from the 2021 short film 'The Silent Echo' went viral on social media, featuring a young girl singing 'Lau Suna Suna' in Nepali about hardship in the mountains.
- **Purpose:** Sen urged listeners to empathise with the broader message about indifference to nature rather than treat the song as a folk elegy miscredited online.

**Important points**
- Sen urged listeners to empathise with the broader message about indifference to nature rather than treat the song as a folk elegy miscredited online.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.13_

#### I'm Game — Screenwriting, film's big villain

**In short:** Dulquer Salmaan's Malayalam thriller 'I'm Game', directed by Nahas Hidhayath, opens with catastrophe and mystery before shifting between spoilt-rich-boy comedy and thriller, with Dulquer acing the high-energy physicality as Dan John. Director Nahas Hidhayath's structural slip-up makes the post-interval portion predictable after the motive is revealed at mid-point, and supporting actors including Myskin, Kathir and Antony Varghese are given little to work with despite an interesting premise.

## Technology

### Top 5 — Technology

#### 1. EOS-05 is India's first dedicated imaging satellite: ISRO Chief

**In short:** ISRO on Friday successfully launched the country's first ever imaging satellite in geo-orbit, placing the over 2,300 kg EOS-05 payload into a precise orbit using a GSLV F-17 rocket from Sriharikota — the 107th launch from Sriharikota and the 19th mission of GSLV. ISRO chief V Narayanan said the 2,367 kg satellite is the heaviest ever launched by GSLV into a GTO or sub-GTO orbit and, once operationalised, will become India's first dedicated imaging satellite from geo-orbit providing "strategic data" for areas including agriculture and disaster management; Prime Minister Narendra Modi and Andhra Pradesh Chief Minister N Chandrababu Naidu congratulated ISRO on the launch.

#### 2. 'Loss of hydraulic systems led AI flight to lose altitude'

**In short:** An Air India Airbus A320 carrying 145 people suffered near-simultaneous loss of all three hydraulic systems while cruising at 36,000 feet on a Phuket-Delhi flight on August 4, triggering an autopilot disconnect, a brief stall warning and an altitude upset that injured 24 people, AAIB said in a preliminary investigation that sought action against the pilot-in-command for testing positive for banned drugs upon landing. The report did not establish that the pilot-in-command's drug abuse contributed to the hydraulic failure or the altitude upset; the aircraft recovered its green, blue and yellow hydraulic systems within seconds, continued to Delhi and landed safely, but caused extensive cabin damage including cracked ceiling panels, dislodged overhead bins and broken handrails.

An Air India Airbus A320 carrying 145 people suffered near-simultaneous loss of all three hydraulic systems while cruising at 36,000 feet on a Phuket-Delhi flight on August 4, triggering an autopilot disconnect, a brief stall warning and an altitude upset that injured 24 people, AAIB said in a preliminary investigation that sought action against the pilot-in-command for testing positive for banned drugs upon landing. The report did not establish that the pilot-in-command's drug abuse contributed to the hydraulic failure or the altitude upset; the aircraft recovered its green, blue and yellow hydraulic systems within seconds, continued to Delhi and landed safely, but caused extensive cabin damage including cracked ceiling panels, dislodged overhead bins and broken handrails.

**What this is**
- **Concept:** 'Loss of hydraulic systems led AI flight to lose altitude'
- **Told:** An Air India Airbus A320 carrying 145 people suffered near-simultaneous loss of all three hydraulic systems while cruising at 36,000 feet on a Phuket-Delhi flight on August 4, triggering an autopilot disconnect, a brief stall warning and an altitude upset that injured 24 people, AAIB said in a preliminary investigation that sought action against the pilot-in-command for testing positive for banned drugs upon landing.
- **Purpose:** The report did not establish that the pilot-in-command's drug abuse contributed to the hydraulic failure or the altitude upset; the aircraft recovered its green, blue and yellow hydraulic systems within seconds, continued to Delhi and landed safely, but caused extensive cabin damage including cracked ceiling panels, dislodged overhead bins and broken handrails.

**Important points**
- AAIB said in a preliminary investigation that sought action against the pilot-in-command for testing positive for banned drugs upon landing. The report did not establish that the pilot-in-command's drug abuse contributed to the hydraulic failure or the altitude upset.
- the aircraft recovered its green, blue and yellow hydraulic systems within seconds, continued to Delhi and landed safely, but caused extensive cabin damage including cracked ceiling panels, dislodged overhead bins and broken handrails.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.6_

#### 3. Teachers use AI to upskill students and themselves

**In short:** With AI in education getting a push from the Union and State governments, teachers across education boards in Bengaluru have started using AI tools to teach children, design worksheets and upskill themselves. Kavitha Ghosh of Delhi Public School, Bengaluru South, uses AI for differentiated worksheets and grammar exercises, noting prompt engineering skills now matter; Samran Wiriyaphong of Canadian International School said the school is launching a year-long Professional Learning Community project called "Assessment Redesign: Building Teacher Confidence in AI-Integrated Classrooms" with AI guardrails, while in elementary classrooms AI serves as a teacher-led demonstration tool and in secondary grades students use AI for exploration while verifying originality.

#### 4. Mid-air scare: Probe flags system failure; AI pilot fired for drug use

**In short:** An Air India Airbus A320 flying from Phuket to New Delhi with 145 people on board last month suddenly lost altitude after all three hydraulic systems collapsed within seconds before recovering, injuring two dozen fliers, the Aircraft Accident Investigation Bureau said in its preliminary report on Friday. The report sought action against the pilot-in-command for testing positive for drugs, calling use of a psychoactive substance by the 34-year-old pilot a serious concern, and Air India terminated his services soon after; the report did not link the pilot's drug abuse to the incident or explain why the hydraulic system failed.

## Opinion & Explainers

### The people who teach us to become (Opinion)

**In short:** India marks Teachers' Day on September 5, the birth anniversary of philosopher and former President Dr. Sarvepalli Radhakrishnan, who is said to have asked students to celebrate all teachers instead of him alone.

- India marks Teachers' Day on September 5, the birth anniversary of philosopher and former President Dr.
- Sarvepalli Radhakrishnan, who is said to have asked students to celebrate all teachers instead of him alone.
- The column argues that classrooms are a child's first real society outside home, that Radhakrishnan saw education as building reason and democratic character rather than rote memorisation, and that teaching today is a complex profession—UNESCO's recent AI campaign stresses technology cannot replace a human paying attention.
- It contends teachers need more than annual cards and speeches: time, training, resources, autonomy and respect, as their influence persists in how people question, choose and contribute long after school ends.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.2_

### Park Bill withdrawal people's victory (Opinion)

**In short:** It is a great victory for all of us as citizens that the government has withdrawn the Park Amendment Bill, which would have allowed 5% of park space to be used for infrastructure and utility purposes, writes Prakash of Bengaluru. Such crucial bills can have a major impact on our environment and green spaces, so there should be more public consultations before they are introduced; protests against the steel factory and efforts to protect Lalbagh have shown that citizens should not be afraid to question or oppose authorities when projects or policies are not in the interest of the people.

### Back step, but in right direction (Opinion)

**In short:** The Karnataka Government's decision to withdraw the bill amending the Karnataka Government Parks (Preservation) Act is a step back in the right direction, writes Richard Lloyd, following outrage from civil society organisations, NGOs and environmentalists against allowing alienation of five per cent of public parks and gardens for infrastructure projects. However, the statement that the bill would be placed in the public domain and reintroduced in the next session suggests the government is not fully convinced of the futility of such a bill; protestors also raised concern over proposed tunnels to allow unrestricted car mobility underneath the city, and the author argues the lasting solution lies in safe, cheap and fast public transport with last-mile connectivity rather than making cities more convenient for cars.

- The Karnataka Government's decision to withdraw the bill amending the Karnataka Government Parks (Preservation) Act is a step back in the right direction, writes Richard Lloyd, following outrage from civil society organisations.

### Kerala's gender debate doesn't need a Gulf alibi (Opinion)

**In short:** Kerala is once again arguing about who controls Muslim women's place in public life, with the debate finding a convenient external villain in the Gulf, but the causal story needs to run in the right direction, writes K.M. Seethi.

- Kerala is once again arguing about who controls Muslim women's place in public life, with the debate finding a convenient external villain in the Gulf, but the causal story needs to run in the right direction, writes K.M.
- Seethi.
- The sequence started with Congress MP Shashi Tharoor posting that the Muslim community needed introspection on the "import of conservative and even reactionary attitudes and customs from abroad," leaning on a World Bank working paper on migrants returning from Saudi Arabia.
- when corrected that the paper's headline claims sat awkwardly against its raw survey numbers, the Gulf-import narrative continued online.
- It was only after Muslim women turned out visibly on Milad-un-Nabi rallies that Kanthapuram A.P.
- Aboobacker Musliyar's faction of Samastha issued a circular instructing that women should not share stages with men, arguing Islam prescribes purdah — an order of events that cuts against the Gulf-import narrative, as Kanthapuram was reacting to the visible, growing presence of Muslim women in Kerala's own religious public life rather than a newly arrived Saudi custom.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.5_

### India joined SCO's Iran statement; BRICS shouldn't be shaped by China-Russia agenda (Opinion)

**In short:** The 26th meeting of the Council of Heads of State of the Shanghai Cooperation Organisation (SCO) has generated photo-ops for the government to reiterate India's ascending status, but India needs to go beyond counting admirations and assess its decisions in global forums, writes Seshadri Chari. At least three aspects of the Bishkek Declaration merit deeper analysis ahead of India hosting the BRICS Summit: India signed a declaration condemning military strikes on Iran as a gross violation of international law, reversing its June 2025 disassociation from a similar SCO statement; the declaration condemned terrorism while Pakistan — an epicentre of terrorism — was a signatory, potentially weakening New Delhi's charge that Pakistan uses terrorism as state policy; and approval of regulations for a Universal Centre for Countering Challenges and Threats is unlikely to yield actionable intelligence sharing given China's dominant position and Pakistan's agenda of opposing India in every forum.

- The 26th meeting of the Council of Heads of State of the Shanghai Cooperation Organisation (SCO) has generated photo-ops for the government to reiterate India's ascending status, but India needs to go beyond counting admirations and assess its decisions in global forums, writes Seshadri Chari.
- At least three aspects of the Bishkek Declaration merit deeper analysis ahead of India hosting the BRICS Summit: India signed a declaration condemning military strikes on Iran as a gross violation of international law, reversing its June 2025 disassociation from a similar SCO statement.
- the declaration condemned terrorism while Pakistan — an epicentre of terrorism — was a signatory, potentially weakening New Delhi's charge that Pakistan uses terrorism as state policy.
- and approval of regulations for a Universal Centre for Countering Challenges and Threats is unlikely to yield actionable intelligence sharing given China's dominant position and Pakistan's agenda of opposing India in every forum.
_Sources: News Trail Mangaluru p.5_

### Real growth, beyond branding (Opinion)

**In short:** NITI Aayog's suggestion to develop a second Bengaluru to ease pressure on the capital comes as the city grew from 40 lakh people in 1991 to over 1.3 crore today, with municipal limits expanding from 69 sq km to over 710 sq km under the Greater Bengaluru framework. Karnataka's GSDP for 2025-26 is Rs 32.81 lakh crore with 8.1% growth, and the editorial argues tier-2 cities such as Belagavi and Shivamogga should become self-sustaining growth centres replicating Bengaluru's opportunities through manufacturing and research rather than residential sprawl alone.

### Why great Indian science misses the Nobel (Opinion)

**In short:** Mani Subramaniam examines why luminaries such as Satyendra Nath Bose, Meghnad Saha and G N Ramachandran never won the Nobel Prize despite breakthroughs including bosons, the ionisation equation and protein structure, while C V Raman won in 1930. The piece notes Mahatma Gandhi was nominated five times without winning, discusses structural limits such as the three-recipient cap, and argues India must strengthen its research ecosystem rather than treat Nobel omissions as the final verdict on scientific merit.

### Building, bridging and balancing (Opinion)

**In short:** Ahead of the New Delhi BRICS 2026 Summit, former BRICS sherpa Sanjay Bhattacharyya recounts India's chairship agenda of building for resilience, innovation, cooperation and sustainability, citing 350 convenings and outcomes in digital agriculture, MSME cooperation, energy standards, and local-currency finance through the New Development Bank. He argues the Iran–Israel–US war tested BRICS unity—Sherpas met repeatedly but deputy foreign ministers could not reach consensus in April, and May's foreign ministers' meeting recorded diverse perspectives on West Asia while agreeing elsewhere—and says BRICS must build crisis-management capacities as members transition from emerging economies to emerging powers with global responsibility.

### Warning on warming (Opinion)

**In short:** The United Nations Environment Programme's Limiting Overshoot report warns that breaching the 1.5°C global warming limit would bring irreversible losses that adaptation cannot undo; even if all countries deliver on climate pledges today, the globe is still poised to heat 1.8°C, and on current policies 2.6°C. The report describes an "overshoot, peak and decline" pathway and emphasises action on methane — responsible for about 0.5°C of current warming — as the most effective near-term lever; over 155 countries have joined the Global Methane Pledge to cut anthropogenic methane at least 30% below 2020 levels by 2030, but India is not among them.

- The United Nations Environment Programme's Limiting Overshoot report warns that breaching the 1.5°C global warming limit would bring irreversible losses that adaptation cannot undo.
- even if all countries deliver on climate pledges today, the globe is still poised to heat 1.8°C, and on current policies 2.6°C.
- The report describes an "overshoot, peak and decline" pathway and emphasises action on methane — responsible for about 0.5°C of current warming — as the most effective near-term lever.
- over 155 countries have joined the Global Methane Pledge to cut anthropogenic methane at least 30% below 2020 levels by 2030, but India is not among them.
- After the Bhotekoshi disaster in Nepal, Foreign Affairs Minister Shisir Khanal said major industrial emitters such as China, the United States and India must consider the impact of rising temperatures on small countries.
- the editorial argues India must take such perceptions seriously and calibrate its messaging to the reality of a 1.5°C world.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.8_

### The Gulf is calling and New Delhi must listen (Opinion)

**In short:** Shashi Tharoor argues that for decades India's West Asia policy operated under deliberate ambiguity and "multi-alignment," treating the region primarily as an economic hinterland, but that posture is becoming obsolete as Washington's "selective engagement" creates a security vacuum and the Mecca Joint Defence Agreement brings together Saudi Arabia, Türkiye and Pakistan at India's western maritime doorstep. He warns that trade ties and diaspora diplomacy alone cannot preserve Indian influence in a region grappling with maritime threats, drone strikes and conflicts involving Iran, Yemen, Sudan, the Strait of Hormuz and the Bab el-Mandab, and that West Asian powers seek hard-power options such as naval patrols, intelligence sharing and defence-industrial collaboration.

- Shashi Tharoor argues that for decades India's West Asia policy operated under deliberate ambiguity and "multi-alignment," treating the region primarily as an economic hinterland, but that posture is becoming obsolete as Washington's "selective engagement" creates a security vacuum and the Mecca Joint Defence Agreement brings together Saudi Arabia.
- Türkiye and Pakistan at India's western maritime doorstep.
- He warns that trade ties and diaspora diplomacy alone cannot preserve Indian influence in a region grappling with maritime threats, drone strikes and conflicts involving Iran.
- Yemen.
- Sudan, the Strait of Hormuz and the Bab el-Mandab, and that West Asian powers seek hard-power options such as naval patrols, intelligence sharing and defence-industrial collaboration.
- New Delhi must evolve from a passive beneficiary of regional stability into an active guarantor through joint maritime patrols, permanent logistics access, interoperable surveillance networks and robust bilateral and minilateral security frameworks with partners such as the UAE.
- Israel and Saudi Arabia.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.8_

### A BIT of a reset, with a wider debate (Opinion)

**In short:** Prabhash Ranjan writes that India is revising its model bilateral investment treaty (BIT) after Finance Minister Nirmala Sitharaman said in the 2025 Union Budget that the 2015 Model BIT would be revamped; the 2015 model tilted heavily toward the state's right to regulate, contributing to doubts among capital-exporting countries and limiting new BIT conclusions. He argues the treaty-making process raises concerns about democratic deficit because those affected cannot participate effectively, and calls for external consultation through a core team of experts, industry bodies and civil society organisations, public comment on a draft as done in March 2015, and placement of the draft model BIT on the floor of Parliament — not as box-ticking but as meaningful participatory democracy including engagement with dissenting views.

- Prabhash Ranjan writes that India is revising its model bilateral investment treaty (BIT) after Finance Minister Nirmala Sitharaman said in the 2025 Union Budget that the 2015 Model BIT would be revamped.
- the 2015 model tilted heavily toward the state's right to regulate, contributing to doubts among capital-exporting countries and limiting new BIT conclusions.

_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.8_

### Crisis of confidence

**In short:** Richard Hay of Kochi, Keralam, writes that India cannot call itself a young nation with confidence if its young people are forced to fight for basic opportunities in quality education, employment, entrepreneurship, economic independence and dignity, and that the government must ask why students are losing confidence in the existing education system. He says every frustrated student on the street represents a failure somewhere in the system, but Gen Z must also understand that democracy requires responsibilities as well as rights and that protests must be peaceful; the government must undertake a ruthless review of Ministries and public institutions where performance is inadequate, with consequences for non-performance, corruption and deliberate obstruction of reforms.

- Richard Hay of Kochi.

### Calling Gen Z apolitical may overlook the issues that genuinely concern them

**In short:** Sodsi Raj of Dhenkanal, Odisha, writes that calling Gen Z apolitical may overlook issues that genuinely concern them, as young people in India engage actively with questions of unemployment, education, climate change and social justice even if they remain distant from conventional politics. The challenge is not merely to increase political participation but to make democratic spaces more accessible and meaningful to young citizens; Gen Z in India may not be apolitical but simply politically disenchanted.

- Sodsi Raj of Dhenkanal.
- Odisha, writes that calling Gen Z apolitical may overlook issues that genuinely concern them, as young people in India engage actively with questions of unemployment, education, climate change and social justice even if they remain distant from conventional politics.

- Gen Z in India may not be apolitical but simply politically disenchanted.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.8_

### The many facets of Mahanayak (Opinion)

**In short:** On Uttam Kumar's birth centenary, N Manu Chakravarthy recalls how the Bengali 'mahanayak' lent poise and grace to roles unlike action-centred heroes elsewhere, even as he openly criticised public expectations that bound him to his image and lamented Bengali cinema's decline after 1969. The essay highlights performances in Satyajit Ray's 'Nayak' and 'Chiriya Khana', Tapan Sinha's 'Upahar' and 'Jatugriha', and 'Antony Firingee', arguing Uttam Kumar transcended script limitations with an introspective, self-reflexive artistry.

- On Uttam Kumar's birth centenary.
- N Manu Chakravarthy recalls how the Bengali 'mahanayak' lent poise and grace to roles unlike action-centred heroes elsewhere, even as he openly criticised public expectations that bound him to his image and lamented Bengali cinema's decline after 1969.

- Tapan Sinha's 'Upahar' and 'Jatugriha', and 'Antony Firingee', arguing Uttam Kumar transcended script limitations with an introspective, self-reflexive artistry.
- Uttam Kumar was born on September 3, 1926.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.14_

### 'UN Convention on the Law of the Sea is still the constitution of oceans' (Opinion)

**In short:** Ramon Magsaysay award winner Tommy Koh, who chaired a pivotal UNCLOS III negotiating committee and as conference president in 1982 led more than 150 nations to adopt the UN Convention on the Law of the Sea, says the award endorses his work to build a world governed by law and not force. In an interview, he says 172 countries are parties to UNCLOS and even the non-party United States regards it as the authoritative law of the sea; Iran is not a party either, but the regime of transit passage has become customary international law and is binding on Tehran, and UNCLOS remains the "constitution" of the world's ocean despite current EEZ and seabed disputes.

- Ramon Magsaysay award winner Tommy Koh, who chaired a pivotal UNCLOS III negotiating committee and as conference president in 1982 led more than 150 nations to adopt the UN Convention on the Law of the Sea, says the award endorses his work to build a world governed by law and not force.
- In an interview, he says 172 countries are parties to UNCLOS and even the non-party United States regards it as the authoritative law of the sea.
- Iran is not a party either, but the regime of transit passage has become customary international law and is binding on Tehran, and UNCLOS remains the "constitution" of the world's ocean despite current EEZ and seabed disputes.
_Sources: The Hindu Mangaluru p.14_

### Bengaluru housing market in 2026: What is shaping the way the city buys homes

**In short:** Bengaluru's residential real estate market is entering a more mature phase in 2026, with healthy demand but increasingly selective buyers weighing connectivity, open spaces, larger homes, amenities and developer credibility alongside Namma Metro expansion and road improvements that are opening peripheral pockets beyond traditional hubs. East Bengaluru (Whitefield and Sarjapur Road) remains strong while North Bengaluru around Devanahalli gains from airport connectivity; buyers now expect Metro access, schools, hospitals and shopping, prefer low-density villas and gated communities over compact apartments, and scrutinise RERA registrations, construction progress and delivery records before committing.

- Bengaluru's residential real estate market is entering a more mature phase in 2026, with healthy demand but increasingly selective buyers weighing connectivity, open spaces, larger homes, amenities and developer credibility alongside Namma Metro expansion and road improvements that are opening peripheral pockets beyond traditional hubs.
- East Bengaluru (Whitefield and Sarjapur Road) remains strong while North Bengaluru around Devanahalli gains from airport connectivity.
- buyers now expect Metro access, schools, hospitals and shopping, prefer low-density villas and gated communities over compact apartments, and scrutinise RERA registrations, construction progress and delivery records before committing.
- Greenery, practical amenities such as co-working areas, sports and pet-friendly spaces, and reliable developers are shaping a market where success depends on choosing the right micro-market and project rather than Bengaluru alone.
_Sources: Deccan Herald Mangaluru p.20_
